A `CI 수정:` line whose conditions do not hold is refused (exit 3) whatever the cap; on `act` the admitted dispatch writes the `CI 처분 처분=수정` row once per head; an implementation whose prompt carries a `CI 합류: <sha>` line is refused (exit 3) unless `다음` is `CI 합류 수정` and the sha is the worktree tip, and while `다음` is `CI 합류 수정` an implementation without that line is refused (exit 3) unless it is a `--resume` dispatch; an implementation whose prompt carries the review fix's `수정 대상: ` and ` 의 P0·P1` is refused (exit 3) while `다음` is `CI 대기`, `CI 재실행` or `CI 대기 한도`; on `act` an admitted `CI 합류:` dispatch writes one `CI 처분` row with `처분=합류` per `(세그먼트, head sha)`, its fields those of the `처분=수정` row, and it spends the cycle budget as a review fix does; a lead fix (`act --kind skill … -- lead-fix`) is refused (exit 3) unless `다음` is `리드 수정` — once its row lands `다음` is the re-check, so one lead fix is dispatched per round; a re-convergence whose prompt carries the scope `설계 수준 잔여 <리포트 경로>` is refused (exit 3) unless `다음` is `재수렴` and the path is the last `cycle` row's report; a review is refused (exit 3) when no implement or lead-fix row follows the last `cycle` row and the segment's worktree HEAD is still that row's `리뷰 HEAD`, so the same HEAD is not reviewed twice; and a review whose prompt carries `--slice` is refused unless the value is the dispatch's `--segment`. A `cycle` row is refused when its `P0` or `P1` differs from the first canonical `발견 요약` line of its report. **The gate stamps `리뷰 종결` on every `cycle` row**, last: `다음` as it will read once that row is the last, with `머지` spelt `수렴` — a new row has no dismissal, no carry-over and no authoring or re-convergence row after it, so the seven values are `수렴`, `수정`, `리드 수정`, `이월`, `잔여 보류`, `판정 불가` and `재수렴`; on a segment with a lead-fix row the new row is a re-check, judged with K counting it. A caller that names it is exit 2, as for `발견 지문`.

**`implement_next[]` is the one value the routers branch on after an implementation ends `정상 완료`.** The snapshot carries one element per segment that has not merged, completed or parked and whose last `stage-result` row reads `종류=implement` with `종단 부류=정상 완료`: `세그먼트`, `다음`, `단계`, `plan_sha256`, `앞선 커밋` and `무커밋 구현`. A **void boundary** is a `stage-result` row with `종류=reconverge` or a `재계획` row; only the implement rows after the segment's last boundary count. `단계` is `A` when the last implement row's `plan_sha256` appears there for the first time after the boundary — a plan emission — `B` when it appeared before, and `미상` when the row carries none; `plan_sha256` is that digest or `-`. `앞선 커밋` is `git rev-list --count HEAD --not <refs>` in the segment's worktree, with `<refs>` the ones of `refs/heads/<base>` and `refs/remotes/origin/<base>` that resolve, `<base>` the `베이스 브랜치` of the segment's target; it is `미상` when the worktree, the base or every ref fails to resolve. `무커밋 구현` counts the `정상 완료` implement rows after the boundary that left no commit: all of them when `앞선 커밋` is 0, only the plan emissions otherwise. `다음` is the first of these that holds: `앞선 커밋` is `미상` → `판정 불가`; `단계` is `A` or `앞선 커밋` is 0, with `무커밋 구현` at most 2 → `구현`; the same with `무커밋 구현` 3 or more → `사람`; otherwise `리뷰`. The array lies outside H and outside the default `--fields`, so a commit in the worktree changes `앞선 커밋` and not H. **A review is dispatched only on `리뷰`**, and the element comes before `review_next[]`: a `확인 리뷰` there does not dispatch the re-check while this element reads `구현`. The routers re-dispatch the implementation on `구현`, park the segment with a cone `blocked` row on `사람` and `판정 불가`, and park it as well when the gate refuses that re-dispatch.

**`stage-lease` records which account a lineage was handed, and it is written before the spawn it covers.** One row per grant, immediately before the stage process starts — never while the routing guard is off, and once it is on, for every grant: a seat grant and a dormant grant get their row before the spawn like any other. Both launch paths write it, the driver's spawn and the gate's stage dispatch; the gate's dispatch passes its lineage itself as `파견 id`, a fixed head that no driver dispatch id begins with, followed by the segment and the attempt number. `계보` is derived from `파견 id` by stripping every trailing `.retry`, never taken from the caller. **Both writers write it in the gate's frame**: the `교대=` seat in front, the chain tip read inside the ledger lock, and `prev=` behind, so a driver row and a gate row for the same arguments are the same bytes and the chain runs through both. An empty value is `-` — a seat grant carries `계정=-`, `예약=-` and `관측=-`, and `근거` (`single-seat` or `shift-seat-fallback`) is what says it was a seat; `계정=-` with any other `근거`, or an account with one of those two, is refused. `예약` is `<five_hour>/<seven_day>`, each component `I.FFFF` — the reservation in basis points over 10000, exactly four decimals, no rounding — and `관측` is `<source>@<epoch>`, `none` or `-`. `비용` is the per-window stage cost the admission used, before the window multiplier, in the same `I.FFFF/I.FFFF` shape, and `비용 출처` names where each window's cost came from — `group-kind` (the measured cell of that quota group and stage kind), `kind` (the measured cell of that kind across groups) or `default` — as `<five_hour>/<seven_day>`. A grant that no admission decided (a seat, a dormant grant, a lease held again) carries `-` in both, and one `-` without the other is refused. `단계` and `단계 출처` are the operating phase the router ordered the grant under and where that phase came from — the cc-lane phase record declared it, the record was absent, a declared burst had expired, or the record was corrupt — and `문턱` is the rest threshold the ordering used, as `I.FFFF` with no ceiling at 1.0000 (one or more integer digits, exactly four decimals), or `-`. A grant the router did not order anew (a seat, a lease held again) carries `-` in all three; `단계=-` comes only with `단계 출처=-`, and a `문턱` other than `-` comes only with `단계=rest`. **Identity values, `그룹`, `레인` and `관측` are refused, never normalised**: an identity is `^[A-Za-z0-9._:#+-]{1,64}$` with at least one alphanumeric and is never `-` or `(미상)`; `계정` and the id of `그룹=acct:<id>` additionally may not have a uuid's shape or be sixteen or more hex characters; `레인` is `~`, or starts with `~/` or `/`, does not end in `/`, and carries no `|`, CR, LF or `@`. So an organisation uuid, an email address or a credential value never reaches a row. **A refusal is rc 2 from the writer and no row at all**; the caller of a refused lease gives back the grant it just received and does not spawn.

**`stage-wait` records one wait, one row per wait chunk.** `계보` as above; `그룹` is `org:<16 lowercase hex>`, `acct:<id>` or `-`; `계정` holds an account only when the wait is bound to one already (`resume-bound-exhausted`, `resume-bound-no-room`, `resume-bound-borrowed`) and is `-` otherwise, and the opposite pairing is refused; `까지` is `-` or the `now_iso` shape `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`; `비용` and `비용 출처` are those of the admission that set the wait — the bound account's for a `resume-bound-exhausted` or `resume-bound-no-room` wait, the chosen group's for a `no-room` or `group-exhausted` wait with a time — and `-` for a wait no admission decided, which includes `resume-bound-borrowed`: that wait is set because the bound account is lent to the cc lane, before any admission runs. Same frame, same refusal rules, same rc 2; the caller of a refused wait parks the lineage. **Every row rewinds the watcher's idle clock**, which is why the chunk period is a decision of its own and not a detail of this row.

**A routed stage dispatch the router answers `WAIT` is waited on; one it parks exits 16, and the row beside it says why.** In a routed run the gate's stage dispatch asks the router before it synthesizes the stage instructions or pins an attempt. A `WAIT` writes one `stage-wait` row for the dispatch's lineage, pins the attempt, starts a supervisor that waits for an account before it spawns, and returns 0 — the key then shows in the snapshot's `waiting_stages[]`. The supervisor asks the router again every half of the effective `STALL`, writes one `stage-wait` row per chunk, and spawns the stage on the first grant. A `PARK`, a resume whose binding the grant does not satisfy — `휴면 구속` when a dormant grant meets a resume bound to a real account — a routing guard that disagrees with the run's record, and a `WAIT` that cannot be waited on (its `stage-wait` row refused, or the effective `STALL` unreadable) each write one act-scope `blocked` row with `사유=라우터 판정`, the stage in `스테이지` and the verdict, reason and recovery in `근거`. A `PARK` whose reason is the deadline writes a cone row instead (below). Either way nothing is launched, so the same dispatch may be issued again once the router grants it: 16 is not an argv or rule refusal (3) and not a reach park (11). In a run that is not routed no dispatch reaches this exit.

**A dispatch of a key already in flight exits 17 before any row.** In a routed run `act --kind skill` holds a per-key dispatch lock, `<run-dir>/<key>.dispatching`, for the length of the act, and refuses — with no ledger row — when another live act holds it, or when the key has a live supervisor, a live stage or a waiting member. `plan --kind skill` shows the same verdict by reading alone. The answer to 17 is `gate.sh wait` on the key, never a second dispatch.

**The waiting marker and `watch.stall` are run-directory files, not ledger rows.** `<run-dir>/<key>.waiting` names the waiting process (`보유자`, with its start-time fingerprint `지문`), who wrote it (`기록자=게이트` or `드라이버`), the `계보`, the `그룹`, the router's `까지`, the last refresh `갱신`, and the stage kind; a key is a waiting member while its holder lives with that fingerprint, no stage is live on it, and no terminal row closes its attempt. A member whose `갱신` is older than the effective `STALL` stays a member — it is never dispatched again — but stops quieting the watcher; the recovery is to end its holder process, after which settlement closes the attempt. `<run-dir>/watch.stall` is three lines the watcher rewrites every pass — its effective `STALL` in seconds, its pid, that pid's fingerprint — and a waiter reads the threshold from it only while that writer lives with that fingerprint, otherwise from the watcher's one shipped default.

**A park a waiter writes carries its attempt's lineage in `근거`.** Every `blocked` row the waiting supervisor writes — the deadline cone, a non-deadline `PARK`, a refused heartbeat — starts `근거` with `계보=B:<key>#<N>`; that is how `gate.sh wait` returns 16 for an attempt that parked before it spawned instead of reading a launch failure, and how settlement leaves such an attempt alone. The deadline park is `스코프=cone 원인=막힘` with `사유=마감 초과 — <key>`, `앵커 세그먼트=<key>` — a segment id, or a run-scope step's id (the design, the audit, the split, the visual stage or the visual publish stage) for that step's dispatch — and a `재개 명령` carrying the router's recovery and the dispatch line to issue again. A cone anchored on a run-scope step's id — this park, or the routing shift's row handing a halt record back to a person — holds the whole run, like a run-scope row, until a row carrying a later attempt's lineage of the same key or a `원인=해소` row of the same `사유` follows it.

**A widened residual, stated rather than closed: the row anchor reaches these rows too.** The `A-<8 hex>` evidence anchor that closes a `problem` obligation resolves by its `prev=` prefix to a row of ANY series, so the two routing series — frequent machine rows that say nothing about a finding — widen the set of rows a rationale can point at. While routing is dormant there are no such rows and the widening is empty.

**`종료 절` and `문서 해시` are listed with the fields the gate actually writes**, because a table that under-reports its writer's series is not a closed definition. The fixed-graph driver writes `문서 해시` rows too, in the same shape and order — `스테이지=<세그먼트, or the stage for a run-scope stage> 이후 | sha256=<the document now> | 동결값=<frozen whole-document digest> | 관측=<time>` — after every stage termination it records, and skips a (stage, value) pair already on the ledger whichever writer wrote it, so that a reader of the ledger takes the latest digest the run recorded.

**The gate's row lookup (`gate_has_row <계열> <키=값> [<키=값>…]`) matches whole fields, not substrings.** Each argument names one `키=값` field; one leading and one trailing `|` and the whitespace around them are ignored, so `"| 키=값 |"` and `"키=값"` are the same lookup. A row answers only when it carries, for every argument, a field byte-identical to it, and several arguments are joined on one row — two fields found on two different rows are not a hit. So a key spelled inside another field's value, a key that ends another key and an id that prefixes another id do not answer, and two fields joined into one argument answer nothing; `scripts/lint-sidecar-field-table.sh` refuses that last shape at the call site. **The answer is sound only while every writer keeps this grammar**, mapping `|` and line breaks out of keys and values alike: a ` | ` spliced in by a writer that skipped that step is a real separator at the byte level, and no reader can tell it from one the writer meant.

**`의무 종결` and `의무 포기` are the exits of a `problem` obligation.** They are separate series rather than one with a state field because they differ in **tense**, and the tense decides when each is checked: `종결` cites a past act row and is never re-verified, `포기` cites a segment being terminal — a reversible, present-tense fact — and is re-verified every time the open set is computed.

Both carry a derived `의무 id` (`PO-<8 hex>` over the run id and the free-text identity) that the reader computes rather than the writer stores, so a `problem` row written before these series existed is closable with no migration. Neither carries the identity verbatim: §3.1a's 1024-byte cap makes unbounded free text unsafe on a row, so `표시 동일성` is a truncated label that **no predicate reads**. `세그먼트` is inherited from the row being closed and never accepted from argv, the same discipline `리뷰 의무` already applies to its carried fields.

the `계획됨` row the router writes on the main checkout before the segment's worktree exists is not that row; no row carrying `브랜치`, or rows naming checkouts of two branches, leaves no record; a tree another segment named earlier is not excluded; a refused terminal row names the segment's own tree, and a record established on a tree the segment did not work in is a halt, not a spelling to copy; a later non-terminal row naming another worktree does not move that record, and one written after a `머지됨` row never becomes it; a segment with no such row — no non-terminal row before its first merge, only rows on the main checkout or inside a checkout, or no branch to pin — has no record, and its `머지됨` row is accepted with its tip unread, so under `선리뷰후머지` its `머지 대조` is `판정 불가` unless no `cycle` row exists, which gives `리뷰 없음`) · `리뷰 정책`(optional, chosen on the segment's first row; a row loosening it against the previous row's resolved policy — the strict default when there is none — is refused on a `머지됨` row always, on any other row once the segment carries `머지 대조`, and on any row once the segment has left planning — an earlier row in a state other than `계획됨`, a `cycle` row of the segment, a `승인` or `자율 승인` row with `kind=skill` naming the segment, or a `stage-result` row of the segment; landing is not asked, because the gate has no fork point of its own to ask it from) · `머지 커밋`(a `머지됨` row) · `고정 커밋` · `적용 명령`(rows the apply act writes) · `적용`(gate-written: `대기` on a `머지됨` row whose apply is still owed \| `미정산` on a `머지됨` row brought in from an earlier run whose apply started and never said how it ended \| `건너뜀` \| `수렴` on the apply supervisor's `완료` row \| `정산` on a settlement's `완료` row \| `사전 실패` \| `불명` on a `park` row; a caller-supplied value is refused. `act --kind apply … -- 적용` writes `적용 준비` and returns, and the detached supervisor it starts writes the ending; `act --kind apply-settle … --cutpoint 배포 --surface 외부상태변경 -- 정산` measures a `적용 준비` with no live supervisor or a `park 적용=불명` with the frozen probe once and writes `완료 적용=정산` or `park 적용=불명 정산 판정=…`; with `--answer <중단 답 줄>` it writes a person's `사람 확인 정산` with no probe; `act --kind apply-settle-confirm … --surface 읽기 -- '중단 답=<줄>'` is a bookkeeping kind graded `읽기` that writes only the `완료 적용=정산 정산 주체=사람` row of a `머지됨 적용=미정산` segment) · `정산 주체`(gate-written: `게이트` \| `사람`, on a settlement's `완료` row) · `정산 근거`(gate-written: the probe code and its mapping, or the `중단 답` line) · `정산 판정`(gate-written: `필요` \| `측정 실패`, on a settlement's `park` row only) · `재실행 근거`(gate-written: the `중단 답` line of a person's `재실행` answer, on the `적용 준비` row that rerun writes) · `감독 토큰`(gate-written: the 64-hex token the apply supervisor's start is bound to, on the `적용 준비` row) · `이전 런`(gate-written: the run id of the ledger an earlier-run import read, on a `머지됨 적용=미정산` row only) · `인가면`(optional, gate-written: `<sha256>→<sha256>` \| `생략(행 길이)` \| `실패(런 디렉터리 없음)` \| `실패(락 대기 초과)` \| `실패(기준선 불일치)` \| `실패(프로브 렌더)` \| `실패(렌더)`; absent means this row widened nothing, a `실패(…)` value means the re-derivation did not land and the next non-terminal row re-derives, and `RUN_DIR/surface-digest` is the current surface either way; never on a terminal row; a caller-supplied value is refused) · `강제 표면`(gate-written on a `계획됨` row from a slice declaration, and driver-written on the planned rows of the slice and planner paths: `<n>건 <분류목록>` — the count of declared files the run hook refuses as an enforcement surface and their classes, comma-separated — \| `없음` \| `판정불가` when the classifier could not be loaded; the paths themselves are not on the row and are recomputed from the declared set wherever they are shown; a reader takes the last row of the segment carrying the field, reading a declared set clipped to its sidecar back whole and a missing sidecar as `판정불가`; a caller-supplied value is refused) · `머지 대조`(gate-written, on `상태=머지됨` rows only: the merge compared against the segment's last `cycle` row — the finding count and whether its `리뷰 HEAD` covers the merged tip — derived whatever `## 룰 설정` says; a caller-supplied value is refused) · `최종 사이클`(gate-written, on `상태=머지됨` rows only: the `사이클` of the `cycle` row compared, or `없음`; a caller-supplied value is refused) · `출처`(gate-written: the run id of an earlier run of the same document, on the terminal row of a predecessor — or of the slice being planned — brought in from that run's ledger; when the row brought in already carried `출처`, that value is kept, so along a chain of runs it names the run that did the work; `매니페스트` on a row brought in from a `선행 착지` row; a caller-supplied value is refused). A caller-supplied `수렴` is refused on this series as well. These three keys are refused by their presence, whatever the value, an empty one included; and a field key carrying a space, a CR or a tab before or after it is refused whatever the key is, on this series and on every other series the gate writes for a caller |

the same test is what the stage reads as `CC_PIPELINE_AUDIT_PASS`, `first` or `reaudit`, exported to an audit stage's launch only and read as `first` when it is absent) · `종단 부류` · `일시 원인`(`예` \| `아니오`, on `종단 부류=크래시` rows only — the gate's row and the driver's S5 row: `예` when the stream's terminal object names a connection error, the stream is not a usage-limit death and that object is not a 429) · `실패 서명`(`<종류>/<종단 부류>/<원인>/<진척>`: `<원인>` the sha256-12 of the stream's last `is_error:true` result event with request ids, uuids and ISO times removed, of the sorted unmet residual headings of a `공허한 성공`, the approval id an attempt was re-attached with, or the halt record's id, and `-` for a usage-limit envelope or nothing to read; absent otherwise) · `출처`(`스테이지 방출` when absorbed from a stage's terminal line) · `finding-id`(required iff `kind=severity`) · `재킥 원인`(end rows, `결정=종료`, only; one of the fourteen tokens below) · `종료 부류`(end rows only: `완료`, on a run whose termination conditions all held; | `리뷰 이월` | `세그먼트`(gate-stamped from `--segment`) · `사이클` · `리뷰 HEAD` · `건수`(the last cycle's P1) · `이슈`(comma-separated issue URLs of the target's repository, never clipped) · `레인`(gate-stamped, tilde form) · `기록 시각`(gate-stamped) |

| `대상 거절` | `대상` · `세그먼트`(\| `-`) · `행위자`(`스테이지` \| `교대` \| `리드`) · `형태`(`감쌈` \| `맨`) · `근거`(`감쌈 예측` \| `예측` \| `추정`) · `판정`(optional: the refusing verdict, `추정` on a settlement row) · `행위 다이제스트`(sha256 of the act's words) · `명령`(clipped at 120 bytes) · `곁파일`(the side file under the run directory holding the full bytes) · `거절 문면`(clipped at 160 bytes) · `조건`(sha256 of the five elements below) · `루트` · `훅 다이제스트` · `HEAD` · `원격 팁` · `PR` · `중단 답`(first 16 hex of the segment's last `중단 답` row, `-` when none) · `기록 시각`(gate-stamped) |
| `원격 판독` | `세그먼트` · `브랜치` · `워크트리 HEAD` · `원격 팁`(full sha \| `없음` \| `미상`) · `PR`(number \| `없음` \| `불일치` \| `미상`) · `PR 상태` · `PR HEAD` · `판독 시각`(gate-stamped) |
| `맨 명령 결과` | `대상` · `세그먼트` · `행위자` · `요청` · `티켓` · `행위 다이제스트` · `판정`(`관측` \| `미상`) · `기록 시각`(gate-stamped) |
| `중단 리포트 건너뜀` | `사유` · `런`(the reported run's id, `-` for a fleet backlog park) · `서명` · `세그먼트`(always `-`) · `기록 시각` |

**The `run` row's three version fields say what code JUDGED the run, and the two beside them say what code was being judged.** `판본` is the pinned commit (`<40hex>` \| `(미상)` \| `(고정 안 함)`), `판본 트리` the pinned plugin subtree's git tree (`<40hex>` \| `(미커밋)` \| `(미상)` \| `(고정 안 함)`), and `판본 다이제스트` the content digest of the copy itself (`<sha256>` \| `(고정 안 함)`). `(고정 안 함)` is not a failure: a run opened before pinning existed, or one opened under the test seam, is deliberately left unpinned, and a reader has to be able to tell that apart from a pin whose value could not be determined. `(미커밋)` means the plugin subtree was dirty when the copy was taken, so no tree object names those bytes.

**`강제 코드`, `베이스 추적 변경` and `베이스 미추적 파일` are about the TARGET BASE, not about the judge** — the base's HEAD at run open and what that worktree held beside it, both read from one `git status --porcelain`. `베이스 추적 변경=있음` means some tracked file had an uncommitted or staged change, so the run acted on code no review saw; `베이스 미추적 파일=있음` means an untracked file was present, which in this way of working is the ordinary state and says nothing about reviewed code. **They are two fields because one predicate over the whole porcelain output answered "dirty" for a base whose tracked files were committed byte for byte** — the untracked side is nearly always non-empty, so the single value could not separate the two nights. `(미상)` on both means the directory or `git status` did not answer; it is never written as `없음`.

**`베이스 검사 기준선` says whether the base's `make check` could have been green at run open**, so a stage that meets a red check at night can tell a failure it caused from one it inherited. The gate runs `make -n check` in the base, takes the tokens ending in `.sh` from the printed recipes, and checks that each exists: `녹색` when all do, `적색(<빠진 경로>, …)` naming the missing ones, `(해당 없음)` when the base has no makefile, `(미상)` when `make -n` itself fails. A token carrying `$` or `=` is an expansion or an assignment and is left out rather than guessed at, so the value answers "is a script missing", not "is the check green". **It is recorded and never refused on.** `-n` runs no recipe, but make still evaluates the makefile's own `$(shell …)` and passes `-n` down to recursive `$(MAKE)` lines. The row is never lost to this field: when the value does not fit `GATE_ROW_MAX` a red value becomes `적색(<n>개 — 행 길이로 생략)`, anything still too long becomes `생략(행 길이)`, and the full value goes to the gate's log. **The driver's fixed-graph path (`run.sh`) still writes the older single field `베이스 청결`** (`예` \| `아니오`) on its own `run` row; the field-table lint below reads only the gate's call sites, and the unattended router does not take that path.

**Restoring a pinned version.** When `판본 트리` is a hash, `git archive <tree> | tar -x` in the plugin repository reproduces the copy, and the result's content digest — computed by the same `pin_digest` the pin used — must equal `판본 다이제스트`. That works even when `판본` names a local commit that a later reset removed, because the subtree it points at is shared with its parent. When `판본 트리` is `(미커밋)` there is nothing to restore from: the copy under the run directory is the only one that ever existed, and once the reaper collects that directory the digest identifies the version without reproducing it. **That is a stated residual, not an oversight.**

**The copy's files are NOT part of the enforcement-surface digest.** The exclusion of plugin files from `gate_surface_digest_raw` stays exactly as it was: that digest's mismatch is exit 7, which is unrecoverable, so a false positive there is the incident that exclusion was introduced to prevent. Two write layers already cover the copy — the run hook's allow-list and the gate's own Bash path guard, both of which refuse every name under a run directory that is not a halt record, a segment plan or a witness product.

**This table is held equal to the gate's call sites by `scripts/lint-sidecar-field-table.sh`.** Per series, the union of literal `<키>=` names across every `gate_append '<계열>' …` call must be listed here; where no call site forwards `"$@"`, the listed set must also be written by some call site. A conditional argument `${v:+"<키>=$v"}` counts as that literal key like any other. `교대` and `prev` are outside the comparison — `gate_append` adds both to every row itself. `stage-result`'s predicate result is folded into `종단 부류`. `인벤토리 기준선` is outside what this lint sees, because its call site is in `run.sh` and not in `gate.sh` — of the series written that way it is the only one reached on the live gate path, the other, `generation`, being written on the driver path alone — so only the driver's tests hold its fields to this row. **No cell carries the `writer pending` marker today, and the mechanism stays written down anyway**: a cell marked `writer pending` is the one shape the lint's reverse direction passes over, and it reports that field by name rather than skipping it silently.

**`재킥 원인` is a closed set of fourteen tokens**: `마감` · `천장` · `무진전` · `사이클예산` · `표면이동` · `연기마감` · `결함` · `판단정지` · `대상미선언` · `슬라이싱` · `시각승인대기` · `말단상한` · `P0잔존` · `해당없음`. A token outside the set is refused by the writer, not recorded. `해당없음` is the default for every ending nobody mapped, so an unclassified stop goes to a person rather than down the automatic branch; `종료 부류=완료` beside it marks the one `해당없음` that is a normal completion.

**A run's end token is the `재킥 원인` of the earliest run-scope row that carries one** — a `자율 승인` row with `결정=종료`, or a `blocked` row with `스코프=run`. Later rows never change it: a run stopped by a surface move keeps `표면이동` even if a later deadline or invalidation writes an end row of its own. "The run has ended" means it has an end token. Every site that writes `done` writes an end row beside it — the boundaries B4 (`천장`, on the router path only: the fixed-graph driver writes no running `cost` row, so B4 has nothing to compare there and `천장` never ends a fixed-graph run) and B5 (`무진전`), the router's deadline branch on `act` and `exec` (`마감`; `plan` writes nothing), and the three arms of a termination proposal (`해당없음`, with `종료 부류=완료` on the arm whose conditions all held). The fixed-graph driver writes its end row at one place every return of its main loop passes, and from its EXIT trap: a stage that halted the run gives `결함` (classification `tool-unavailable` or `precondition-failed`, with `재킥 지문`) or `판단정지` (`gate-unanswerable`); a walk that landed every slice gives `해당없음` with `종료 부류=완료` — a slice an earlier run landed and this run brought in counts as landed, and one it skipped because an earlier run's pull request is still open does not; a walk that ended on slice parks gives the first of their `대상미선언`/`슬라이싱`/`시각승인대기` tokens — the last is the park of a screen slice whose screens the visual sidecar does not record as approved for implementation, written before its branch is cut; anything else gives `해당없음`. Its first deadline park writes `마감`.

**`P0잔존` is decided at one place, inside the gate's end-row writer, from the ledger and the manifest alone.** When a caller hands it `해당없음` and `종료 부류` is empty or `완료` — never `포기` or `이어받음` — the writer asks whether the run stopped only because the re-check review cap left P0 findings: the manifest declares a design document; at least one segment's last `cycle` row counts more than zero `P0`, its re-check rounds counted again from the ledger exceed that segment's cap, and its last `segment` row is `park`; every other segment the ledger has a `segment` row for is `머지됨`, `완료`, such a segment, or a dependency park whose `미완 선행` names only such segments or other dependency parks counted the same way; and no approval is open. When all of that holds the row is written with `재킥 원인=P0잔존`, without `종료 부류`, and with the per-segment `재킥 지문` of the field table; otherwise nothing changes. A dependency park without `미완 선행` — a router run's — is not counted, so such a run stays `해당없음` and goes to a person. The `done` file's first line follows the same decision: `P0 잔존 — 확인 리뷰 상한에서 P0 가 남은 세그먼트: <목록>` in place of the completion line.

**A `P0잔존` successor chooses its first step once.** A `P0잔존` successor's first step, before the design step, chooses between re-converging the frozen document on the P0 findings and redesigning from the interview record, records that choice as a `자율 승인` row with `kind=rekick-branch`, and on a restart reads the row back instead of choosing again.

**`gate.sh end-overdue --manifest <path>`** closes a run that passed `벽시계 마감` with no end token, once; a second call writes nothing. The token follows whether the driver is alive: a fixed-graph run whose fleet marker `busy/<id>.<pid>` names a live process takes `마감`, a router run with a live shift or stage takes `마감` only when neither progress axis is declared (a declared axis ends that run itself, so nothing is written), and a run that stopped or cannot be observed takes `해당없음`. The backlog record's state is never evidence that a run is alive.

**`gate.sh end-abandoned --manifest <path> --cause <이어받음|포기> [--successor <run id>]`** closes a run that no boundary will end: another run took it over (`이어받음`), or a person gave it up (`포기`). It writes `done` and one end row through the same first-write rule every boundary uses, so a second call — or a call on a run that already has `done` — writes nothing. The row carries `재킥 원인=해당없음`, because neither cause is a reason to kick the run again automatically, and `종료 부류=<cause>`, which says which of the two it was; with `이어받음`, `--successor` names the run that took over and the row's `근거` carries it. **Only the seat in the person's turn, or a kickoff taking over an earlier run, calls it**: a call carrying a stage's or a shift's marker is refused, and so is a call on a run that still has a live shift, a live stage or a fresh waiter — that run is still working, so nothing is written. `--cause` and `--successor` are refused on every other verb.

**The run's watchers leave on their own once the run waits for nobody, and that is not an ending.** `watch.sh` and `checks.sh` both stop when `cc_run_idle_exit` in `liveness.sh` holds: no live shift, live stage or fresh waiter; no open approval; no halt record that a `중단 답` row has not answered or that the shift handed back; and a ledger that has not grown for `CC_RUN_IDLE_EXIT_SECONDS` (24 hours, declared there once and nowhere else). The growth clock is the latest of the heartbeat's `마지막성장`, the ledger's mtime and the moment that watcher started, so a watcher started again on a quiet run watches a full threshold before it leaves rather than reading the stamp its predecessor left. Neither writes `done` or a row on that exit — the run stays open, and closing it is `end-abandoned`. This is a separate predicate from `버려짐`, which never reads `halt/` and so would read a run waiting on a person's answer as abandoned after an hour.

**Every declared series has a writer, except one — and that exception is the rule holding rather than an omission.** A series that nothing writes turns the check reading it into a constant. `stage-lease` and `stage-wait` have a writer on both sides — the gate's `gate_append`, reached through its two envelope wrappers, and the driver's `ledger_row` — and, in a routed run, call sites: both launch paths write a `stage-lease` row before each spawn, and both write a `stage-wait` row when the router answers `WAIT` and then one per chunk while they wait. In a run that is not routed no code writes either.

They are written from three different places, because the three have different knowledge. `run` is written once at run open, by the gate. `stage-result` and `cost` are written by the gate's detached stage supervisor when a stage terminates, from the stage's **own** terminal result line — its cost, its subtype, its session id — so nothing here depends on a stage reporting anything about itself. `problem` is an `act` kind like `segment` and `cycle`: recognising that a finding is the same finding as last cycle's is a judgment, and the router is where judgment lives. The two routing series add two more places: `stage-lease` is written on the launch path, before the spawn it covers, and `stage-wait` from the wait loop, once per chunk — the gate's waiting supervisor or the driver — after one first row written synchronously by the dispatch that received the `WAIT`, so the previous stage's terminal row does not stay the ledger's last for a whole chunk.

**`stage-result` has two writers, and the row says which.** The gate's supervisor writes the rows of stages it dispatched (and its prelude the settlement rows), the driver writes the rows of stages it spawned itself and the stage-less apply rows; `기록자=게이트` or `기록자=드라이버` names the one that wrote this row. Both carry `압축 창` — the compaction window the launch read, with the layer it came from — and `레인`, the CLI config home the stage ran under, so a reader can tell a stage compacted under the lane's default from one the gate handed a window on its argv. The layers are read in one fixed order on both paths: the window the gate injects on the argv (`argv`; the driver injects none, so a driver row never says it), the run's per-kind settings file (`런설정`), the stage cwd's `.claude/settings.local.json` then `.claude/settings.json` (`프로젝트`), the lane's `settings.json` (`레인`). The two keys are merged per key across those layers, the way the CLI merges settings: `autoCompactEnabled` is taken from the highest layer that defines it and the window from the highest layer that holds an integer, each on its own. `(꺼짐)` is that highest `autoCompactEnabled` reading false — it wins over the argv value too, and then nothing is injected — `(미상)` a layer whose file exists but could not be read (every layer is read before anything is decided, so an argv value never stands in for a failed reading), `-` no layer at all. The launch leaves the two values in `<seg>.window` beside the pid record so a settlement that runs after the supervisor is gone can still put them on the row — **and that file is never a precondition of anything**: a settlement or a collection that finds it absent writes `압축 창=(미상)` and proceeds, and its presence is not a liveness input. A row with none of the three keys was written before they existed; that absence is a third state and is not read as `(미상)`. **`계정` is a fourth key whose absence means something else**: the stage was launched without a routing account, which is every row written while routing is dormant. The launch writes the account on the fourth line of the same `<seg>.window` record — the third line is the effort the launch put on the argv, which `effort` below reads — and the driver's rows and the gate's settlement rows read it through one parser, while a gate supervisor whose launch was routed writes the account its launch token carried, held from the moment it took the token, because the record is named by the segment and a later attempt of the same segment can rewrite it; the same rule applies to that value: no fourth line means no field, `-` is a seat, an id that passes the identity rule of the two routing series is written as it is, and anything else is `(미상)` — the row is never refused for it, because losing a terminal row would lose what `--resume` authorisation and settlement de-duplication read. **A resume is bound to the account on the rows that recorded its session.** The binding reader reads only rows whose `세션 id` is exactly the session (a value without a session id's shape, such as the `미상` the gate writes for a stage with no terminal result line, is refused before the ledger is read): one account binds to it, `-` binds to a seat, no field or `(미상)` binds to nothing, and rows that disagree — two ids, or an id beside a seat or beside no binding — are refused rather than resolved. A dormant `GRANT` carries no account to compare, so it lets a resume through only when the binding is empty or `-`; a resume bound to a real account is never carried by a dormant grant. The gate injects a window for one stage kind (`review`) today, and `CC_ORCH_STAGE_AUTOCOMPACT=off` switches that injection off for every kind without touching the settings layers. Every stage launch, the shift launch and every driver judgment call also carry a `--model` and an `--effort` read from one table by kind. The model is `opus` for every kind but the shift; the shift gets `CC_ORCH_SHIFT_MODEL` when it is `sonnet` or `opus` and `sonnet` otherwise (any other value is ignored), and no other kind reads that switch. The effort — `design`, `reconverge` and `audit` get `high`; `implement`, `review`, `shift`, `generic` and the judgment calls `triage`, `segment-plan` and `redesign-impact` get `medium`. `CC_ORCH_STAGE_EFFORT=off` drops `--effort` alone and `CC_ORCH_STAGE_EFFORT=<kind>:<level>[,…]` overrides the listed kinds (`low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` or `max`; any other level is ignored); `CC_ORCH_STAGE_MODEL=off` drops `--model` alone for every kind, the shift included, and any other value is ignored — only the shift's model is set, by `CC_ORCH_SHIFT_MODEL`. The row's `effort` is the value that went on the argv and its `서빙 모델` is what the stream's first frame says answered; the context window is never read from that model's `[1m]` suffix, only from `modelUsage.<id>.contextWindow`. `교대 기동` carries the same `레인` and `압축 창` for the successor routing session, with its `세션 id` to join on, and `모델` — the model the launch requested on the argv, `-` when `CC_ORCH_STAGE_MODEL=off` dropped it; a shift is not a stage kind, so it never gets an argv window.

**`handoff` has two writers too, and `사유` says which.** A shift writes its own through `act --kind handoff` with `상한`, `승인`, `종단`, `중단`, `대기` or `이월`. **`이월` says the run waits for its next run, not for a person**: a person's halt answer found no account with room before the deadline and its `이월` row keeps it for the next run. It is accepted only when no stage or supervisor is alive, no halt record waits for an answer, at least one answer with an `이월` row is still unspent, the run has no ending row (a shift whose carry came after the deadline closed the run hands off `종단`), and the row's `CI 대기` is `-`; otherwise it is refused with exit 3 and no row. Before the deadline the seat call's relay waits on it until one second past the deadline; past the deadline the same act writes the run's `마감` ending row. **`중단` is refused when an `이월` answer is all that holds the run** — the conditions above hold, nothing else is open, and no answered halt waits to be handed on — and when its `막힌 지점` names the record or the file name of an answer that has an `이월` row: the seat would ask the person again for an answer already given. **`종단` is accepted only from a run that has ended** — `$RUN_DIR/done` exists, or the ledger carries an ending row (`결정=종료`, or a run-scope `blocked` row) from which the end token is read; otherwise the arm refuses with exit 3 and writes no row, and the shift hands off with `중단`. **`대기` hands the wait to the gate**: the shift ends because all that is left is a live stage's result or a pending CI verdict, and the gate process of the seat's `router-shift` call waits in the shell and launches the successor when what it waits on moves. The arm derives the wait set itself — every live stage and supervisor, plus the segments the row's `CI 대기` field names, a comma-separated list of segment ids — and refuses a `대기` with an empty set with exit 3 and no row. `CI 대기` counts toward the row's length but is never clipped, because clipping it would shrink the wait set without a word: a row that does not fit with the free-text fields at their floor is refused with exit 2 and no row. Every shift-written row carries the field, `-` when no segment is named. The gate writes one more after it reaps a shift that wrote none: `사유=무기록`, `버린 선택지=-`, `다음 후보=-`, a fixed descriptive `막힌 지점`, and two fields no shift's row carries — `종료 코드`, the shift's exit, and `행위 수`, the count of `자율 승인` rows with that shift's `교대=<n>` and `행위자=교대` — and a third, `일시 원인`, `예` when the shift's own stream ended on a dropped connection and `아니오` otherwise. The snapshot's `handoff[]` elements carry it as `일시 원인` (`-` on a row without the field). **A `예` row is relaunched without a person**: the next shift launch after it probes the connection first — at most three tries, after waits of 30, 60 and 120 seconds — and records `연결 확인=<tries>/성공` on its `교대 기동` row; a probe that never answers, or a fourth such launch in a row, is refused with exit 3 and no row, and the run waits for a person as before. **A person's turn skips both**: a launch with no `교대 기동` row after the last `handoff` and, besides its own `자율 승인` row, at least one more `kind=router-shift` `결정=act` row after that `handoff` — an earlier attempt that started nothing — is launched without the probe and without the bound, and its `교대 기동` row carries no `연결 확인=`, so the count restarts. The seat issues that second attempt only on a person's word, never on a channel wake. Whether the shift wrote one is read from those approval rows (`| 교대=<n> | kind=handoff |` with `행위자=교대`), whose `교대` and `행위자` come from the environment and not from argv. The `handoff` arm refuses `무기록` on argv, so the value means the gate wrote the row. The trace is written only on a path where the gate outlives the shift; a gate ended with the shift leaves none.

**`generation` is deliberately still unwritten.** Nothing reads it, so the writer arrives with the reader or not at all.

**`stage-lease` and `stage-wait` are the stated exception to that rule**, and the exception is narrow. No code reads either series yet, and their writers land anyway because the routing design requires the rows to EXIST, not merely to be readable: a lease row before every spawn, so the ledger says which account each stage ran on, and a wait row carrying its group, so the morning can count which groups blocked. That a person reads the ledger is not the ground — it is equally true of `generation`, and a principle whose exception is "somebody might read it" no longer separates any series from any other.

**The terminal class the gate writes is a strict subset, and the omission is deliberate.** From outside a stage it can distinguish `크래시` (a non-zero status, a subtype that is not success, or an envelope that declares `is_error`), `한도 종료` (split off `크래시` inside that arm: a non-zero exit whose own pinned stream shows the stage died of the usage limit, judged by the rule in §5.2), `정상 완료` (the stage performed at least one gated act — a `자율 승인` row stamped `행위자=스테이지` for that segment, landing after that attempt's dispatch row; router rows written while the stage ran do not count), `산출물 없는 정지` and `공허한 성공` — the last two separated by whether the stage's own transcript carries a `permission_denials` entry, which is precisely the "trace of reaching a decision point" this contract asks for. `의도된 park` is read from the stage's halt record, which the halt contract owns. `거절 종료` sits between `의도된 park` and `크래시`: the last `result` envelope with a positive `num_turns` carries `stop_reason` `refusal`, read with the driver's own reader, so it is filed at either exit status and never folded into `크래시`. An implement stage on its process-B turn that would otherwise be `정상 완료` is `공허한 성공` when a residual item of its own share is still graded `구현 시 검증` with no W2 line (§5.2). `적용 불명` alone is **not** written from here: it is a claim about an apply step's outcome, and its only writer is the driver's apply-outcome-unknown arm.

**`외부 종료` is written by a different path and is defined by an absence.** Every class above presumes the stage emitted a `type=result` envelope and reads that envelope's fields. `외부 종료` is written by the gate's prelude — on every verb but `plan` — when it settles a lost dispatch: a record the gate itself dispatched (`<seg>.kind` present, a non-empty fingerprint, `<seg>.attempt` present) whose CLI process and supervisor are both gone, with no `stage-result` row for that attempt. Its defining check is that no envelope exists, so it is disjoint from `공허한 성공`: a CLI that exits 0 having produced nothing is still reporting, while a process that simply vanished reports nothing. **It is the one class the gate writes about a stage it never classified**, and it does not name which outside force ended the stage. A settlement row carries `종료 코드=-` and `부모=-` (the settler is not the session that dispatched), a prose `관측`, and no accompanying `cost` row — there is no envelope to read a cost from. Exactly one `stage-result` row exists per `(세그먼트, 실행 버전)`: the settlement looks for an existing row before appending, and a driver-spawned record (no `.kind`) is never settled, because the driver collects it itself.

**`segment` and `cycle` have a writer on the router path, and both are `act` kinds rather than a seventh verb.** `act --kind segment` and `act --kind cycle` take **`키=값` fields after `--` instead of a command**, because what they perform *is* the row; they grade `읽기`, since a row reaches nothing a cutpoint or a credential could widen. A `segment` row is refused without a `상태` in the vocabulary below and without a `워크트리` — the merge rule enters that directory to read the branch's current HEAD, so a row missing it turns a review refusal into one that names a missing worktree. A row in a non-terminal state is further refused unless that `워크트리` is free of double quotes, backslashes, pipes and control bytes and is an absolute path inside an existing worktree of some declared target (§2b.1), and it is the row on which the gate re-derives the stage settings and writes `인가면`; a terminal row (`완료` · `머지됨` · `park`) is exempt from that check and carries no such field. A `머지됨` row whose worktree tip has not landed on the base is refused with exit 2, and so is a `완료` or `park` row whose `커밋` has already landed — unless the segment already has a `머지됨` row, or the commit is contained in a sibling's `머지됨` row that carries a `커밋` — so a merge cannot be recorded under a state the merge comparison does not read. A `segment` row that would cross the row cap with the gate's fields is refused before the append with exit 1, naming the longest caller field, the projection and the cap. A `cycle` row is refused without `사이클`, `P0`, `P1`, `리뷰 HEAD` and `리포트 경로`, which are exactly the five that rule reads — the last one because the merge rule opens that file and looks for a findings summary, so the merge rule refuses a segment whose newest row points at a stub report rather than believing it; the `cycle` writer itself accepts a complete row whose report cannot be opened (check 8 below passes a `전체`/absent row in that case). The `cycle` writer also refuses, with exit 2 and no row written, any caller field whose key — leading and trailing blanks stripped — is `세그먼트` (whatever its value, an empty one included), whose key as written differs from that stripped key, or whose stripped key is `발견 지문` or one of the eleven cause fields (`계기` · `수정 경로` · `앞 HEAD` · `리뷰 HEAD 고정` · `리포트 형식` · `항목` · `비불릿` · `앵커 없음` · `태그 밖` · `요약` · `HEAD 줄`); stderr names the key. `세그먼트` is written by the gate from `--segment`.

**A `cycle` row may carry `모드` and `기준 사이클`, and a delta claim is refused at write time on eight checks.** `모드` is optional and **absence reads as `전체`** — in the gate's basis search, in the snapshot's `cycles[]` consumer, in the router and in check 8 alike. A delta review read only the files changed since this segment's last full cycle and re-adjudicated that cycle's P0/P1, while the merge rule reads `P0`/`P1` off the newest row without knowing the mode, so everything that makes the claim sound is refused before the row exists rather than believed after it. **Checks 1–7 run only on a `모드=델타` row** (check 1 whenever the field is present at all): (1) `모드` is `전체`, `델타` or `해소` — a `해소` row is held to the resolution checks under the base overlay table instead of checks 3–7; (2) `기준 사이클` is present and a positive integer — digits only, compared everywhere below **by integer value**, so `04` and `4` name the same cycle — and the row's own `사이클` is an integer **greater than** it, so a delta cannot name itself or a later cycle as its basis; a `모드=전체` (or absent) row carrying a `기준 사이클` is refused, since a full review asserts no basis; (3) a `cycle` row of the **same segment** exists whose `사이클` **equals** `기준 사이클` as a field value — never a `grep -F` substring, since `사이클=1` is a substring of `사이클=10` — and when several rows share the number, the last `전체`/absent one among them is the basis, falling back to the last of them only when none is full, so a delta row cannot shadow the full row beside it; (4) that basis row's `모드` is `전체` or absent, so a delta never stacks on a delta; (5) the basis is the **largest** `전체`/absent `사이클` of this segment by integer value, and the refusal names the one that is; (6) inside the segment's last `segment` row's `워크트리`, `git merge-base --is-ancestor <기준 행 리뷰 HEAD> <이 행 리뷰 HEAD>` exits 0 — exit 1 (not an ancestor) and exit ≥2 or a missing worktree (undecidable) are refused **with different wording**, because folding 128 into "not an ancestor" would read a missing object as merely unrelated; (7) the basis row's `리포트 경로` resolves to a file that exists and matches `^[-*[:space:]]*\*\*발견 요약\*\*`. **Check 8 runs on every `cycle` row**: the row's own `리포트 경로` is opened, the one line matching `^[-*[:space:]]*\*\*리뷰 모드\*\*: (전체|델타)( |$)` is anchored first and the values are read off that line only; no such line reads as `전체`, no `모드` on the row reads as `전체`, and the two must agree; when both say `델타`, the line's `기준 사이클 <n>` must equal the row's `기준 사이클` by integer value, and the line's `` 기준 리뷰 HEAD `<sha>` `` and the basis row's `리뷰 HEAD` must resolve — `git rev-parse --verify <x>^{commit}` in the segment worktree — to the **same commit**, so a short and a long sha of one commit agree and an unresolvable one is refused as undecidable. A report that cannot be opened refuses a `델타` row and passes a `전체`/absent one: no new refusal is added to the existing path, and that absence is what the merge rule already catches at merge time. Check 8 runs on full rows because the dangerous direction is **under-claiming, not over-claiming** — a report that says `델타` under a row that stays silent reads as `전체`, is picked as the next cycle's full basis, and a delta then stacks on a review that read part of the tree. Both sides absent read as the same `전체`, so no row or report written before these fields existed is refused. Every refusal here is exit 2, and **the repair depends on which check refused**. A mode mismatch is repaired by rewriting the row to the mode the report says. Every other refusal of a delta row that still stands once the row's `사이클` is this cycle's number and its `기준 사이클` matches the report line is repaired by **no** row — rewritten as `델타` it meets the same check again, and rewritten as `전체` it meets the mode comparison, because the report still says `델타` — so that refusal's wording says so, and the router re-dispatches the segment's review as a full review without the three basis flags and writes no `cycle` row until a report the gate accepts exists. A relative `리포트 경로` is resolved the way the merge rule resolves it — two directory levels above the manifest — by one helper the gate uses for checks 7 and 8 alike.

**A `cycle` row records its cycle's canonical report, and says why the review ran.** A segment's reports are numbered by its `cycle` rows, resolutions included: the n-th review writes `docs/reviews/seg-<run id>-<segment>-cycle<n>.md` under the same base a relative path resolves to, and a re-dispatch of that review `…-cycle<n>-r<k>.md` with k ≥ 2; that is the path `gate.sh review-line` prints. A `cycle` row whose `리포트 경로` resolves to any other path is refused with exit 2 and no row, after every other refusal; a review dispatch (`act` or `plan`, `--kind skill`, stage `review`) whose prompt's `--report-path` is not that path, none included, is refused with exit 2 too. The same dispatch declares `--trigger '<token>'`: a token the ledger's facts stand behind for that segment (`첫 리뷰` when it has no `cycle` row, `리뷰 P0`·`리뷰 P1` from the last row's counts unless a dismissal or carry-over took them, `CI 실패`, `베이스 충돌`, `버전 경합`, `리베이스`, `해소 리뷰`, `중단 답`, `중단 인계`), or `라우터 판단` with a non-empty `--trigger-reason` only when none does; anything else is exit 2 with no row and no park, and the refusal prints the facts. A recovery dispatch declares none and inherits its first dispatch's; `--trigger` anywhere else is exit 2. The facts are read from rows of this run only, so a cause that happened before the run is invisible to them. Behind the caller's fields the gate then writes the eleven cause fields of the shape table, measured against the segment's last `cycle` row before this one becomes it. **`리뷰 HEAD 고정=예` means the reviewed commit is held by the ref `refs/cc-cmds/review/<run id>/<segment>/<40-char sha>`** in the repository of the segment row's `워크트리`, written after every refusal so a refused row leaves no ref, and skipped (`건너뜀`) where that worktree is missing or foreign, or the repository is a mirror or pushes `refs/cc-cmds/`. Nothing in the pipeline lists or deletes these refs; a person removes them by prefix, a month or a run at a time, from that repository: `git for-each-ref --format='delete %(refname)' refs/cc-cmds/review/ | grep '^delete refs/cc-cmds/review/202610' | git update-ref --stdin` — the prefix goes to `grep`, because `for-each-ref` matches a literal pattern only up to a slash.

**The shape table above carries the fields `gate_record_row` REQUIRES and the fields the rules READ, and it has to carry both.** It is the router's only instruction for what to put on a row: **a field some rule reads that is absent from this table is a rule that cannot fire, and that failure is indistinguishable from the check passing.**

**`segment.리뷰 정책` is optional, and omitting it on a later row is INHERITANCE rather than a reset.** The gate carries the previous row's value forward **at write time**, so every row states the policy in force at that moment and the last row alone answers the question. A value exceeding that target's `리뷰 정책 상한` is **refused with exit 2 and never clamped**: a quietly tightened row is indistinguishable from a conservative one. The carry is on this field alone and deliberately not on `워크트리`, which is required on every row and so cannot be erased in the first place.

**`절단점` and `유도 절단점` are two different claims and are carried as two fields.** `--cutpoint` is what the CALLER said, and the gate also derives a rung from the argv itself — `gh pr merge`, a non-GET `gh api …/pulls/<n>/merge`, `…/pulls/<n>/merge-async`, `…/merges` or a write that moves a branch without a pull request — `…/git/refs…`, `…/contents/…`, `…/merge-upstream`, each a base change no binding can hold, as the GraphQL ref writes are — (read with its `?query`, `#fragment` and trailing `/` removed, and with short options read letter by letter as gh reads them, so `-iXPUT` is a PUT, and with flags ahead of `api` skipped the way gh skips them; an `api` word whose place as the command cannot be told reads as such a merge), a `gh api graphql` body naming `mergePullRequest`, `mergeBranch`, `enqueuePullRequest` or `enablePullRequestAutoMerge`, and a non-GET `gh api graphql` body in a file (`-F query=@<file>`, `--input`), which names nothing the gate can read, are `머지`, `gh pr create` and its alias `gh pr new` are `PR`, `git commit` is `커밋`, `terraform apply`/`destroy` is `배포`, and wrappers (`lockf`, `command`, `find -exec`, `rg --pre`) are unwrapped to whatever they run — except that a `gh` call inside a `find -exec` whose words carry `{}` is `머지` whatever it names, because find puts each match's path in place of `{}` and the endpoint that runs is not the one written. `절단점` on the row is the rung the act was **adjudicated** at and `유도 절단점` is what the argv said, `-` where the table has no row for that command. **Declaring below the derived rung is refused with exit 8**; declaring above it passes and the derived rung wins, because labelling every act with the target's cutpoint is the ordinary router path. **`git merge` and `git branch` are deliberately not derived, and `git push` is derived from what it does rather than from the table** — whether a push IS the merge depends on which remote refs it updates, so the gate strips wrappers and git's global options, runs `git push --dry-run --porcelain` with the same arguments in the act's directory, and derives `머지` when a base update appears, for every spelling the dry-run can reproduce (`heads/`, `-c`, `-C`, `:`, a glob, `--all`, `--mirror`, `--branches`, a configured push refspec). The push options reach the dry-run through an allow-list, and on a target with a base branch a push it cannot reproduce — inside a shell `-c` string, `env -S` or `sudo` where the gate finds `git … push` in that text or cannot show the text runs no push (it names `git`, carries an expansion, a glob or a group, or a command word is off the gate's short list of commands that run no text of their own), inside a `find -exec` whose words carry `{}` (the dry-run would push to a literal `{}`, the real push to each match's path), behind an environment assignment other than `LANG`, `LC_*` or `TZ` (`HOME=` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=` included) or an `env` option, with a command-running `-c` key or `--config-env`, or with an option outside the allow-list, abbreviations included — is refused with exit 2 before the rules run and leaves no derived rung. **That derivation also lowers a higher declaration**: a `배포`-declared base push is adjudicated at `머지`, where the review binding opens, and every sha it would put on the base must be the segment worktree's HEAD. When the dry-run names no ref, the refspecs are read instead and `:`, a glob, `--all`, `--mirror` and `--branches` count as base pushes of the local base ref. **A residual, stated rather than hidden**: when the derived rung wins, both fields carry the same value, so an act declared honestly low and one declared high and pushed down read alike on the row — the difference survives only as a `과신고` line on stderr.

**`자격` records which credential each act actually ran under.** With neither pipeline credential provisioned the gate falls through to the ambient one — on a developer machine a full-scope login — and the fallback is kept, and `주변` on the row is how the morning tells a run that had the separation from one that only appeared to.

**`행위자` records which seat wrote the authorisation row — `리드`, `교대` or `스테이지`.** Nothing else on the row could: `교대=` is `0` for the lead and for a stage alike (it reads only the shift marker), and no guard stops a stage from calling `act --kind skill`. The progress vector's `dispatches=` component reads this field to decide whose dispatches count: it counts `stage-result` outcomes (`정상 완료` · `의도된 park` · `산출물 없는 정지`) only for a segment whose dispatch authorisation row carries this field with a value other than `스테이지`, which is what keeps the constrained side from writing its own progress — and the authorisation row alone, which lands before the launch, moves nothing. The act budget's spend count reads it the same way. A row written before the field existed carries none and contributes nothing — the safe direction. The field does not replace the runtime judgment (the gate reads the seat markers from the environment at judgment time).

**`승인` advances by appending, never by editing** — the same discipline `segment.상태` already takes (§3.4). A row carries the `승인 id` it advances; readers take the last row for an id as current. Everything needed to re-issue the question after a session cut is on the row.

**`절단점` on a `승인` row is not always a cutpoint token.** Three shapes share the series because they share the lifecycle: an **act** approval carries a `CUTPOINTS` token and a binding tuple of `(대상 별칭, 슬러그, 행위 토큰, argv 다이제스트, 브랜치, head_sha, base_sha, PR 번호, 리뷰 리포트 다이제스트, 열린 P0·P1)`; a **judgment** approval carries the literal `판단` and a tuple of `(스테이지 id, 질문 문면 다이제스트, 선택지판, 스냅숏 다이제스트 앞 12자)`; a **boundary** approval — issued by B1–B4, which have no act at all — carries the literal `경계` and a tuple of `(경계 이름, 그 경계 술어가 자기 안에서 계산한 결속값)`. Staleness is re-derived at execution against whichever tuple the row carries, so the three do not need three series.

**The judgment tuple is serialized into the one `구속 튜플` field as four `/`-separated components** — `<세그먼트>/<질문 전문의 sha256>/<선택지판>/<스냅숏 다이제스트 앞 12자>`, e.g. `S2-slice-A/3f1a…(64 hex)/v1/298c3e07b3c6` — the same shape the act tuple already takes, and no separate `질문 다이제스트` or `선택지판` field exists. The question digest is of the FULL question text, which the approval sidecar (§3b) holds under the block the row's `사이드카 앵커` names, so the row's digest has something to be compared against. `선택지판` is a version token (`v1`) rather than a digest, because the option set is a compile-time constant of the gate; it says which menu the row MEANT. It does not prove what a person saw; that comparison is made at close, by re-deriving the labels from the gate's table and matching them against the transcript's `options[].label`. Where the segment is unknown the first component is `-`.

**The boundary tuple's second component is the value the boundary's own predicate read** — B1 the progress digest, B2 the digest of the obligations its count is waiting on and that are not yet disposed (the ones open when the count last restarted, less every identity in the disposition latch — so a new identity opened and closed again, or a parked segment toggled, does not move it), B3 the act-budget window key (the progress vector with `acts=` removed; the vector carries no obligation component), B4 the progress digest until a bucket width is decided — and the approval id is `<경계 이름>-<sha256(RUN_ID + 경계 이름 + 결속값) 앞 8자>`. `RUN_ID` stays in the salt so the same condition in two runs does not share an id. There is no clock component: `발행 시각` is the only time on the row. Duplicate suppression is "the last row for that id is `대기`", not "any row exists" — a resolved id re-opens with a fresh `대기` row when the same binding value recurs, and the row sequence says what happened to it.

**A pending approval has two ends, not one.** `무효` is reachable through the same transcript binding as `승인`, so a person can answer *this should not have been asked* without also granting the act. Voiding **removes a blocker**, so it does not get a looser gate — it keeps the requirement that the answer be a real answer frame in the harness-written transcript.

**The transcript binding is by FRAME, not by text.** The line that closes an approval must be the `tool_result` of an `AskUserQuestion` call whose question carried the approval id (joined through `tool_use_id` to the `tool_use` block in the same transcript) and must hold the harness's `toolUseResult.answers` map, in which the question text is the key and the person's choice is the value. The id is looked for by containment anywhere in the question text. Nothing else qualifies: not the router's own Bash output echoing the ledger, not another tool's result, not an `is_error` frame (a dismissed dialog — the harness's text `The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use` — or a collapsed call; the two get different warnings because they are opposite evidence about whether a person was present, and the first is called `다이얼로그 취소`, never `기각`, which is a state token). An ineligible frame, a torn last line, or a line that carries the id and is no frame at all leaves the approval `대기` and writes **no row**. Between two answer frames for one id the later one wins, and the search stops at the first lineage transcript holding one.

**For a `절단점=판단` approval the answer is read by label equality against the gate's own label set, never by scanning prose.** The gate owns the labels (`승인` · `거부` · `무효`) and the router renders them verbatim; the person's choice is compared whole-string, in **normal form** — the label with everything from its last ` ← ` onward removed, because the AUQ authoring rule marks the recommended option by appending ` ← 추천` to the label itself — and a menu whose normalized labels are not exactly the gate's set is refused before any answer is read. An answer equal to a label closes the approval with that state. **An answer equal to no label is FREE INPUT and is neither a grant nor a refusal** — except on a `사람-결정` approval, where free input closes it `상태=승인` with `처분 사유=자유 입력` and the answer bytes go to `answer/<id>.md`, because there the person's words are the instruction the router carries out. A `시각-승인` approval is the other way round: its `승인` label only reports an approval the design document's visual sidecar records, so `close` refuses that label (exit 3, nothing written) while `visual-check.sh approval` does not hold, and free input on it stays `대기` like any other class. On every other class the approval stays `대기`, the answer's full text goes to the approval sidecar only, and a further `대기` row is appended carrying `처분 사유=자유 입력` (or `슬롯 부재` when the frame's question slot has no entry in the answers map), `응답 토큰`, `사이드카 앵커` and `관측 시각` — and NO answer field, because `gate_approval_field` returns the last value a key ever had, so an answer on a `대기` row would read to every later reader as an answer with no mark of its status. `close` exits 5 on that path, not 0: 0 means resolved. `--void` and `--reject` may only agree with the label the person chose; a flag against it is refused. Act and boundary approvals have no menu and no label set, so for them the frame decides and the closer's flag is the disposition, as before.

**`질문 문면` and `답변 문면` are excerpts; the full texts live in the approval sidecar.** A row must stay inside the row-length cap of §3.1a, and a question with four option descriptions does not. `질문 문면` is clipped to 400 bytes on every row that carries it; `답변 문면` on a judgment approval's `승인`·`거부`·`무효` closing row is clipped to 160 bytes (the `무효` closing row is the thinnest and keeps roughly 61 bytes of headroom); `사유` on a `철회` row to 120. Both clips are normalized before they land — `|` and newlines would splice the row grammar — and cut with a visible marker rather than silently. Beside each excerpt the row carries the sha256 of the full text (`구속 튜플`'s second component for the question, `답변 다이제스트` for the answer), and `사이드카 앵커=<run-id>#<승인 id>` names the sidecar block those digests are of — without the anchor the digests would be values recorded and compared against nothing.

**For a `절단점=판단` approval, `답변 문면` carries the answer BYTES (as that excerpt); act approvals keep the fixed literal `트랜스크립트 판독`.** For an act approval the answer is binary, so the literal loses nothing; a question's answer is what the next step consumes. Closing rows of every shape also carry `응답 토큰` — the `tool_use_id` of the answer frame — and `답변 다이제스트`.

**Auto-resolution is the other closing path with no person behind it, and it says so on the row.** Unless `CC_CMDS_AUTOPILOT_AUTO_RESOLVE` is off, the gate closes the approvals that carry a recommendation the moment it issues them: a boundary approval as `승인` (continue), a judgment approval as the adoption of the router's own submitted judgment when its class may be adopted — inside the judgment vocabulary and not `팀-구성`, `시각-면제` or `설계-골격`, the classes that hand risk to the user — and as `거부` otherwise, including a class outside the vocabulary and a judgment carrying no class. Act approvals are never auto-resolved. Nor are three kinds of `대기`: a B4 at or above the declared cost ceiling, which is left open because nothing else stops spending, an approval whose last row carries `처분 사유` (`자유 입력`, `슬롯 부재`), which a person has already answered, and a judgment approval of a held class — `사람-결정` or `시각-승인` — which only a person answers: it is held, not refused, so its act exits 5. **A `시각-승인` has a predicate arm instead**, run whatever the switch says: when `visual-check.sh approval` holds on the design document and no person has answered the question in words, the gate closes it `승인` with `처분 사유=자동 해소` and `답변 문면` naming the predicate, and the same act adopts it. **In a run whose plan holds the visual publish step `S2P`, the predicate also proves where the line came from**: the line ends with the tail ` — 사람 답 <record file name>`, the tail names the run's latest `시각-판정` record of `S2P` (the largest attempt number among `halt/S2P#<n>.md` whose `**스텝**` has that prefix), and a `중단 답` row with `답한 주체=사람` and `선택지=승인` answers that record; a line without the tail is not adopted in such a run. Every caller of the predicate — this arm, the screen slice's dispatch refusal, `close`'s `승인` label check and the driver — gets the same verdict. **A person's `거부` to `시각-판정` and `발행하지 않음` to `발행-실패` close it instead, and that is not auto-resolution**: the gate writes the closing row inside that same `중단 답` act — `상태=거부` or `무효`, `답변 문면=중단 답 연동(기록 <file>, 선택지 <answer>)`, `처분 사유=중단 답 연동` — and a `시각-승인` of the same document submitted later is closed the same way where it is submitted, on the same `중단 답` row. A reader takes such a row as the person's answer, never as an autonomous decision. **Every closing row — this path's and `close`'s — is appended under a transition guard**: inside the ledger lock the gate re-reads the id's last row and writes only if it is still `대기` (or `철회`, for `close`) and, for this path, carries no `처분 사유`. A refused auto-close writes nothing; a refused `close` fails rather than landing a second closing row whose last-row-wins reading would overwrite the first. The `대기` issue row is still written, and the closing row carries `답변 문면=자동 해소(추천: …)`, `응답 토큰=-`, `답변 다이제스트=-` and `처분 사유=자동 해소`, so a reader never mistakes it for a transcript answer. **Closing a boundary approval — by a person or by this path — restarts that boundary's count** in the run directory; without that the next evaluation re-issued the same question with the same count.

**`철회` is the one closing state with no answer behind it, and it has no clock.** A boundary approval whose raising condition has gone away is withdrawn by the gate's own boundary evaluation — never by the router, which has no verb for it — with `사유=` naming the condition that lapsed; the transition is refused while an answer frame for the id exists in this run's lineage, and while a `다이얼로그 취소` was observed in the same cycle. A later real answer may still close a `철회` approval: `close` admits `철회` as the one non-`대기` starting state. The entry point that writes `철회` lands with the boundary predicates; this vocabulary accepts the token ahead of it.

**`대상 추가` records a repository the run reached that the manifest did not name, and it is a RECORD rather than a grant.** A row that conferred a cutpoint would move the seat of authorization from the manifest to a file the run writes. A widening of the stage settings by a segment's own worktree is **not** this series — it is recorded as `인가면` on the `segment` row that caused it (§2b.1, the paragraph opening "The stage's authorization list is re-derived"), so every row here names an actual repository with an actual alias.

So the row's `층` is `0` or `1` and never higher. Layer 0 is read-only — clone, fetch, read, run that repository's tests — already reachable with arbitrary bash, so refusing it buys nothing and recording it buys the morning report. Layer 1 is local commits and branches, capped at `브랜치`, and the cap is **hardcoded rather than inherited or chosen**: nothing above `브랜치` leaves the machine, so no approval is needed and the split-writer rule is untouched. A layer-1 row is admissible only after the same preflight a manifest target gets — the main worktree exists and the common git directory matches — because stash attribution is per-REPOSITORY rather than per-worktree.

`push` and above take neither path. They park with the cause `대상 미선언`, and the resume command is the re-kickoff that writes a successor manifest.

**`리뷰 의무` exists because `선머지후리뷰` defers an obligation rather than removing one.**

**`생성 등급` on this series is read by nothing.** The excusal rule reads a field of the same name on `problem` rows; this one is kept because the morning wants to know what kind of act deferred the review.

**`머지 커밋` is the commit BEING merged and not a commit the merge creates** — the tip of the segment worktree at issue time, so it and a `cycle` row's `리뷰 HEAD` both name commits that exist before the merge does.

**`대상` and `이행 판정` are written by the gate in the carried position where the router's argv cannot overwrite them.** `대상` is the target alias the merge was authorized against, and the fulfilling side runs its landing test in **that** target's anchor repository rather than in the segment worktree. `이행 판정` is a **closed three-value field**: `착지·포함` (the merge landed on the base and a review covers that commit), `미착지` (it has not landed), `앵커 없음` (the row predates `머지 커밋` and cannot be measured). There is no fourth value and there is no `판정 불가` — an unanswerable probe refuses the fulfillment, so it leaves no row to carry a value on. `근거` is required on the fulfilling row on the same terms the `blocked` arm demands it.

**The row-length budget, restated as the number a writer can use.** The cap is 1024 bytes (§3.1a). The longest fulfilling row with `근거` empty measures 399 bytes, so what remains for `근거` is **625 bytes**.

**`실행 버전` belongs to `stage-result`, not to `segment`.** The session uuid is derived from `owner-doc|구간|단계|시도`, so `시도` must be durable — otherwise a reboot re-derives a uuid already bound to a different transcript. Attaching the field to the per-stage row is what makes that durable at the right granularity.

**`stage-result` is what removes the last edge into stage-owned ledger writes.** Its every field is observable by the driver from outside the stage: an exit code it waited on, artifacts it can stat, a digest it can compute. Nothing here requires the stage to report anything.

**`세션 id` and `부모` are the ancestry record, and without them the implementation-review separation rule is vacuous.** Recording the id the harness actually assigned, plus the id of the session that spawned it, makes the rule a real ancestry-closure check rather than a comparison of derived ids — and a fork inherits its parent, so a forked session cannot review its own work by taking a new id.

**`handoff` holds an abandoned alternative**: a successor shift starts from the snapshot alone, which carries progress, not *what was tried and dropped, and on seeing what*. The row takes `키=값` fields after `--` like `segment` and `cycle` do, grades `읽기`, and — like `blocked`, `종료 절` and a judgment `자율 승인` — does **not** require `--segment`: a shift is an event of the whole run rather than of one segment. Each of the three free-text fields is clipped to 300 characters, which is what keeps the row inside the cap of §3.1a.

**`계측 필링 건너뜀` is the twenty-fifth because a filing that did not happen has to be told apart from a round with nothing to file.** The gate runs the metrics collector from its prelude (§3.6) and turns what fired into at most one GitHub issue; when it cannot — no observation Project number configured, no write-scoped credential, a GitHub call that failed or an identity that is not the configured account, an instrument issue of the same base already open — the trigger would otherwise vanish, and silence reads in the morning as "nothing was wrong". The filings that DID happen go on `자율 승인` rows with `kind=metrics-filing` and `절단점=필링`, because they are decisions the run took. Both rows carry `세그먼트=-` and **no `행위자` field**: a metrics round is an event of the machine's runs rather than of any one segment, and the terminal classifier's fourth condition reads `행위자=스테이지` together with the segment, so a filing row spelled with a stage actor would be counted as a stage's own act on a segment that does not exist. `트리거` is the fired signatures (`<id>/<종류>`, comma separated), clipped like any free text — never the close list, which is what the collector judged gone rather than what fired. **A round that carries closes and nothing fired writes no skip row**, whatever prerequisite it then finds missing: there was nothing to file, so "could not file" is not true of it, and because the collector restates a close every round for as long as the condition stays gone, a skip row written there would recur six-hourly for ever and be byte-identical to the real signal.

### 3.3 Closed vocabularies

| Field | Values |
| --- | --- |
| `자율 승인.kind` | `lane` \| `citation` \| `severity` \| `visual-waiver` \| `verification-residual` \| `audit-composition` \| `unresolved-issue` \| `refinement` \| `roster-degradation` \| `stage-retry` \| `target-expansion` |
| `자율 승인.판단 부류` | `문서-신선도` \| `감사-발견` \| `심각도-조정` \| `잔여-항목` \| `인용-갱신` \| `스테이지-재시도` \| `팀-구성`(pre-adoption forbidden) \| `시각-면제`(pre-adoption forbidden) \| `설계-쟁점` \| `설계-골격`(pre-adoption forbidden) \| `구속-이탈` \| `사람-결정`(pre-adoption forbidden; person-only — the gate never answers it and holds its approval at `대기`) \| `시각-승인`(pre-adoption forbidden; person-only — held at `대기` like `사람-결정`, closed `승인` only while the design document's visual sidecar carries the implementation approval, and free input on it stays `대기`) |

| `자율 승인.절단점`(filing row) | `필링` — a marker outside the ladder, on the same layer as `판단` and `경계` of `승인.절단점` |

| `자율 승인.담기` | `성공` \| `실패` (`kind=halt-report` rows) |

| `segment.머지 대조` | `충족` \| `이월` \| `기각` \| `메타 해소` \| `위반(카운트)` \| `위반(미포함)` \| `위반(카운트·미포함)` \| `리뷰 없음` \| `판정 불가` \| `정책 면제` (gate-written, `상태=머지됨` rows only) |
| `segment.최종 사이클` | a positive integer \| `없음` (gate-written, `상태=머지됨` rows only) |
| `target.리뷰 정책 상한` | `선리뷰후머지` \| `선머지후리뷰` \| `리뷰없음` (optional; absence reads `선리뷰후머지`) |
| `대상 추가.층` | `0` \| `1` |
| `blocked.사유` | `도달 park` \| `인가 한도` \| `사다리 R4` \| `사다리 단 부재` \| `사이클 예산 소진` \| `리뷰 잔여` \| `자동 채택 미달` \| `자동 채택 불성립` \| `예산·벽시계` \| `게이트 park` \| `시각 정합 park` \| `외부 상태 불확정` \| `대상 미선언` \| `강제 표면 이동` \| `라이브니스 침묵` \| `스테이지 종단 후 라우터 무응답` \| `세그먼트 미개시` \| `인벤토리 스냅숏 손상` \| `인벤토리 기준선 루트 불일치` \| `살아 있는 인벤토리 손상` \| `인벤토리 검사 불가` \| `인벤토리 스냅숏 게시 실패` |
| `blocked.스코프` | `act` \| `cone` \| `run` |
| `자율 승인.등급 출처` | `표` \| `불투명` \| `미상` (exec rows) |
