| `자율 승인.선언` | `-` \| a `SURFACES` token — what `--surface` claimed, where it differs from the graded value or is the only grade there is |
| `자율 승인.표지` | `-` \| `비밀출력` \| `파괴` |
| `자율 승인.파괴 출처` | `-` \| `유도` \| `신고` \| `유도·신고` |
| `자율 승인.도달` | a `REACHES` token \| `-` |
| `자율 승인.유도 도달` | `-` \| `기기전역` \| `배포트리거` \| `런로컬` (the last only where the first three answer `-` and argv proves the act run-local: a `mktemp`·`mkdir`·`rm`·`mv`·`cp`·`touch`·`ln`·`chmod` whose every path operand resolves physically under the run directory or under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/cc-proto-<run id>-<stage id, # as ->.*`; it replaces the declared reach in the disposition, and `도달` still records the declaration) |
| `자율 승인.식별자 대조` | `-` \| `미선언` \| `일치` \| `대조불가` (`불일치`·`부재` park rather than land on a row) |
| `blocked.도달 판정` | `비밀출력` \| `신고등급한도` \| `도달미상` \| `기기전역` \| `push원격불일치` \| `도달모순` \| `dev파괴` \| `dev식별자불일치` \| `dev식별자부재` \| `dev대조불가` \| `파괴형태미명시` \| `prod인가없음` \| `배포트리거인가없음` \| `CI실패` \| `등급회귀` \| `등급회귀판정불가` \| `재실행배포트리거` \| `재실행대상미상` \| `재실행요청실패` \| `라벨인정없음` \| `라벨새실행없음` \| `라벨배포트리거` \| `라벨이미있음` \| `라벨요청실패` \| `PR라벨쓰기` |
| `target.dev 식별자` | optional; `<종류>:<값>` elements separated by `,` — `aws-profile` \| `aws-account`(12 digits) \| `kube-context` \| `host` \| `domain` \| `dir`(absolute) |
| `target.배포트리거 식별자` | optional; same shape — `branch` \| `workflow` \| `jenkins-job` \| `argv` |
| `handoff.사유` | `상한` \| `승인` \| `종단`(only once the run has ended — `done` or an ending row; refused with exit 3 otherwise) \| `중단` \| `대기`(refused with exit 3 when nothing is live, no `CI 대기` segment is named and no `리셋 대기` key is named; as a launch reason, accepted only while the latest `handoff` is `대기` or `이월` with no `교대 기동` after it, or is the gate's `무기록` for a shift whose own stream yields a reset clock within the horizon, with no `교대 기동` after it) \| `이월`(refused with exit 3 while a stage or supervisor is alive, a halt record waits for an answer, no answer with an `이월` row is unspent, the run has an ending row, or `CI 대기` is not `-`; past the deadline the same act writes the run's `마감` ending row) \| `무기록`(gate-written only; never a launch reason and never accepted on argv) |
| `blocked.원인` | `막힘` \| `무효화` \| `불명` \| `판정 불가` \| `거절 소진` \| `해소` |
| `종료 절.상태` | `충족` \| `불가능` \| `보류` |
| `stage-result.종단 부류` | `정상 완료` \| `의도된 park` \| `공허한 성공` \| `크래시` \| `적용 불명` \| `산출물 없는 정지` \| `외부 종료` \| `한도-형상 회수` \| `한도 종료` \| `거절 종료` |
| `stage-result.기록자` | `게이트` \| `드라이버` |
| `stage-lease.근거` | `first` \| `sticky` \| `resume-bound` \| `reassigned-after-limit` \| `reassigned-no-room` \| `after-wait` \| `shift-seat-fallback` \| `single-seat` |
| `stage-lease.단계` | `rest` \| `burst` \| `-`(a grant the router did not order anew) |
| `stage-lease.단계 출처` | `declared` \| `absent` \| `expired` \| `corrupt` \| `-`(only with `단계=-`) |
| `stage-wait.근거` | `group-exhausted` \| `no-room` \| `unknown-concurrency` \| `fifo-yield` \| `resume-bound-exhausted` \| `resume-bound-no-room` \| `resume-bound-borrowed` \| `lease-lock-busy` \| `lease-contention` |
| `압축 창.출처` | `argv` \| `런설정` \| `프로젝트` \| `레인` \| `꺼짐` \| `미상` (the parenthesised token of `압축 창` on `stage-result` and `교대 기동`; `-` carries no source) |
| `segment.상태` | `계획됨` \| `실행중` \| `리뷰중` \| `머지됨` \| `완료` \| `적용 준비` \| `park` |
| `generation.segmentation` | `ok` \| `low-confidence` |
| `checks.상태` | `대기` \| `통과` \| `실패` \| `미등록` \| `판정 불가` |
| `checks.필수 집합` | `없음` \| `판정 불가` \| check names joined by `, ` |
| `CI 처분.처분` | `재실행` \| `수정` \| `라벨` |
| `CI 처분.상태` | `요청` \| `수락` \| `실패` \| `-` (a `수정` row) |

**`판정 불가` appears on BOTH `checks` fields, and the duplication is the point.** `미등록` is the value a reader takes as "this repository reports no checks", and a call broken by auth, network or a rate limit would otherwise fall straight into it — so "the question broke" and "the question was answered and the answer is none" would be one value. `필수 집합` carries the same split one level down for the same reason: this repository answers a non-zero exit code for "nothing is required", so the exit code alone cannot tell that apart from "a required check failed", and reading the latter as the former hands a broken PR to a merge. Both are the reason the merge-time consumer treats `실패` with `필수 집합=판정 불가` as no verdict at all.

**`blocked.도달 판정=재실행배포트리거`, `=재실행대상미상` and `=재실행요청실패` come from the rerun act `act --kind ci-rerun`, and no `사전 인가` row opens any of them.** The act parks at the merge-check seat, through the ordinary reach-park dispatch, when a failing run it would rerun may start a deploy — a workflow, run or branch named among the target's `배포트리거 식별자`, a workflow whose `on` keys the gate cannot prove are only `pull_request` events, a base workflow fired by the check chain, or a run whose own workflow file, read line by line, carries an `environment` key, calls a workflow under `.github/workflows/`, starts one (`workflow run`, `/dispatches`), runs a script under that directory, or holds a shape the reader cannot judge — a comment, a paths filter item naming a workflow file and a workflow path inside a `run` body do not count — (`재실행배포트리거`), when a run cannot be proved this pull request's own or read at all (`재실행대상미상`), and when an earlier request of the same head is still open or closed by `실패`, or GitHub did not take the POST or the confirmation did not land (`재실행요청실패`). `관측` names `<check>:<run id>`. The repair line never suggests a row: the first two are a person's judgment of the workflow, and the third points to GitHub. Each of the three repairs is a person rerunning the run on GitHub and answering the segment's `도달 거부` halt with `재시도`, or a new commit moving the head. A rerun a person started by hand with no such answer is read as round 0 of the same head; with a person's `재시도` answer, the next rerun act records it as this head's rerun round. Every other refusal of the act is exit 3 with no row.

**`blocked.도달 판정=라벨인정없음`, `=라벨새실행없음`, `=라벨배포트리거`, `=라벨이미있음` and `=라벨요청실패` come from the label act `act --kind label-ack`, and no `사전 인가` row opens any of them.** The act parks at the merge-check seat the same way as the rerun act: when the failing check is not named by both the target's `라벨 인정` row and the segment slice's `라벨 인정` field (`라벨인정없음`), when the failing check's own workflow at the head does not take `labeled` on `pull_request` or cannot be read (`라벨새실행없음`), when a workflow the label event could wake — or a base workflow fired by the check chain, or the PR's head branch or the POST argv among the target's `배포트리거 식별자` — may start a deploy (`라벨배포트리거`), when the label is already on the PR while the check still fails (`라벨이미있음`), and when an earlier label request of the same head is still open (cause `open`) or closed by `실패`, or GitHub did not take the POST or the check screen did not change (`라벨요청실패`). The repair line never suggests a row, and the gate never puts the same label on twice: a person confirms on GitHub whether the label is on and the check ran again; a new run that passes raises the round and the merge goes on through L2 or L5.

**`blocked.도달 판정=PR라벨쓰기` is exec's refusal of every label write on an existing issue or pull request number**, whatever reach is declared, whether or not auto-resolution is on — with it off and no reach declared the act still parks here and is never issued as an approval — and whether or not the target has a `라벨 인정` row — `gh pr edit`·`gh issue edit` with `--add-label`·`--remove-label`, `gh pr create` and its alias `gh pr new` with `--label`·`-l`, a non-GET REST call on `…/issues/<n>/labels` or below, a non-GET REST `…/issues/<n>`·`…/pulls/<n>` carrying a `labels` field or an opaque body, a GraphQL mutation that changes labels, and `gh api graphql` whose query comes from outside the argv. It is judged before the lifting by a `사전 인가` row, so no row — a form row included — opens it: an unattended run puts a label on a PR only through `act --kind label-ack`, and hands anything else to a person. `gh issue create --label` and the label definitions (`gh label …`, `repos/<slug>/labels`) stay on the collaboration surface.

**`blocked.도달 판정=등급회귀` and `=등급회귀판정불가` come from the merge-time grade regression probe, the CI verdict's sibling in the same seat.** On an act the gate adjudicates at `머지` that leaves the worktree or integrates history — the same narrowing the CI verdict uses — the gate builds the merge result tree itself with `git merge-tree --write-tree <base ref> <segment tip>` (no fetch; the base ref is the tracking ref when the target has an origin), and when that tree differs from the base under `plugins/cc-cmds/orchestrator` it runs `scripts/grade-regression-probe.sh` taken **from the base ref**, so the tree under test cannot replace its own checker (only while the base does not carry the probe yet is the landing tree's copy used, with a warning; when neither carries it the verdict is `등급회귀판정불가`). The probe extracts the plugin from both trees, stands up a throwaway run for each (its own repository, manifest, authorization record and state home) and asks each tree's `gate.sh grade` for the same fixed argv list, each child under `env -i` so nothing the calling gate exported reaches it. A known grade before the merge that becomes empty or `등급 미상` after it is `등급회귀`; a probe that cannot decide — its positive control `true` not graded `읽기` by the base tree, a conflicting merge, an extraction that failed, a child that timed out — is `등급회귀판정불가`. Both park through the ordinary reach-park dispatch, so `plan` forecasts them with the same code. A change between two known grades is reported and not refused. The segment side is the head the landing argv names — the REST merge's `sha` field, or `--match-head-commit <sha>` — when it names one, and the segment worktree's tip otherwise (a `gh pr merge` names a head that binds only together with `--admin`; without it the gate's binding check has already refused the argv as unbound, because on a base that requires a merge queue it enables auto-merge or enqueues, so the head is compared at the request and the merge happens later); a named head that does not resolve to a commit in the target is `등급회귀판정불가`, never a fallback to the local tip. A merge whose segment side leaves the orchestrator directory as it was at the merge base (as it is on the base, when there is no merge base) costs a `git merge-base` and a recursive `git diff-tree -r` and runs nothing — a release commit that only bumps `plugin.json` is such a merge; that question is asked before `merge-tree`, so a history that cannot be merged but touches no orchestrator file is not parked here. **The driver's own merge function does not call the probe** — the unattended router merges through the gate, and that is the path this covers.

**`승인.상태` has six values and the gate's `APPROVAL_STATES` constant is their single source of truth; `scripts/lint-approval-state-vocabulary.sh` holds the two equal.** `기각` is written by nothing today and stays in the set — the table is the authority and does not drop a value for being unobserved (the paragraph below says why). `철회` is written by the boundary evaluation that withdraws an approval whose raising condition went away — there is no clock and no router verb reaching it. The dismissed-dialog transcript event is called `다이얼로그 취소` precisely so it is never confused with `기각`.

`blocked.사유=강제 표면 이동` and `=라이브니스 침묵` are written by the gate's surface check and by the watcher's stall transcription, and `segment.상태=완료` is accepted by the gate as a terminal state. The five `인벤토리 …` values are written by the driver's `rundir_init`, which the gate calls before every verb, so they are preamble rows written on any verb, `plan` included; while a block is open each value keeps one unresolved run-scope row, and duplicates added by concurrent entries fold into it; an `act --kind blocked` carrying `원인=해소` gets through only after the cause is gone, because `rundir_init` runs ahead of the verb. **Nothing is REMOVED from the table for being unobserved**, and that asymmetry is deliberate: a declared value that no artifact carries means "not seen yet", not "does not exist", and deleting it would make the next writer improvise a synonym.

**`자율 승인.kind` does not carry a classification.** On the gate path the field holds the **act kind** the decision was attached to, and on an `exec` call it is empty; the table's eleven tokens are what the legacy fixed-graph writer declared, kept for reading old rows.

**`자율 승인.판단 부류` is where a classification actually lives.** On a field that also carries the act kind, one manifest line reading `종류=skill` would pre-adopt every stage dispatch there is; a new field has zero legacy rows, which is what lets the lint assert the closed set with no exception.

**The three forbidden values are IN the vocabulary and forbidden there, rather than left out**, so the leak arrives as a refusal instead of a borrowed permitted token. The refusal is at **freeze time**: `check_manifest` compares every `자동 채택` row's class against the ten and hard-stops on any of the three. Recording a judgment OF that class is still permitted — what is forbidden is pre-adopting it.

**`segment.선행` and `segment.선언 파일 집합` are carried by the router and consumed by the gate; neither is authored by either.** The authority is the design document's slice declaration. `선행` is the cone's declared axis — the only axis that sees a dependency before the predecessor merges — and `선언 파일 집합` is the sole input to "did this segment reach outside what it declared", a question git cannot answer at all.

Two floors sit on `선행`, enforced at write time because `선행` is an *input* to the derivation the cone's superset check compares against. **It is monotone per segment id** — a later row may add and may not remove. And **absence is not `없음`** — in a repository carrying two or more segments a `segment` row with no `선행` is refused, while `없음` is accepted as a positive statement of independence.

**`segment.머지 순서` is the second kind of predecessor: it orders the merge, not the dispatch.** `선행` says the slice builds on the predecessor's work, so the slice is dispatched only once that work has landed. `머지 순서` says only that the slice reaches the base after the predecessor does — the two may be implemented at the same time, and only the merge waits. The slice declaration states it as `**머지 순서**: 슬라이스 X, …` or `없음`, naming slices declared earlier, never a slice its `선행` also names; a declaration carrying the line also carries `**선행 사유**` when `선행` is not `없음` and `**머지 순서 사유**` when `머지 순서` is not `없음`. Because the two may be implemented together, a slice's declared files must not overlap — as path prefixes, with no exemption — the files of any slice it reaches through `선행` and `머지 순서` together, except a slice it reaches along a path that begins with `선행`, whose work it builds on anyway. A violated rule is a defect of the declaring slice: that slice and the slices that depend on it park, and the rest of the run goes on.

**The field is the gate's.** Only the planning act that fills the row from the frozen document (`--from-declaration`) writes it; a caller's `머지 순서=` on any `segment` row is refused with exit 2. Every later row carries it forward at write time — minus the tokens of that row's own `선행`, because an implementation predecessor also lands before the merge — so the last row alone answers. Three floors hold: `선행` stays monotone as above; **`선행 ∪ 머지 순서` is monotone per segment id**, so a token may move from `머지 순서` to `선행` and may not leave both; and every `머지 순서` token names a segment this ledger knows, refused at write time like a `선행` token. The cone's declared axis reads the union.

**That last floor orders the planning.** A slice is planned only once every slice its `머지 순서` names has a `segment` row on this ledger — a predecessor still held back by its own `선행` has none — while a predecessor an earlier run or a `선행 착지` row landed needs no row from the caller, because the planning act brings it in first. Planned too early, the act is refused with exit 2, and when the token names a slice the frozen document declares and whose `레포` is a target of this run, the refusal says so: the slice is declared and has no row yet, which is the order of planning and not a missing landing. The remedy is to **defer** this slice until that slice has a row — a wait, not a stop, and no new run is needed. When that slice is itself held back by a `선행` that has not landed, the refusal names those tokens, because then it cannot be planned first either and the row appears only once they land. Any other token — one the document does not declare, or a declared slice whose `레포` is not a target of this run — meets the unknown-token refusal, which names the two ledgers it looked in and the manifest's `선행 착지` row, the one thing that brings such a predecessor in.

**The merge waits for the order.** On an act at the `머지` rung or above that lands on the base — a remote merge argv, a `git push` git says updates the base branch, or a command the grading table cannot read that writes outside the worktree — of a segment with a row, the gate refuses with exit 3 while any token of the last row's `선행 ∪ 머지 순서` names a segment none of whose rows is `머지됨`, or `완료` other than one brought in from an earlier run without a hex `머지 커밋`. It writes no row and no park cell — the refusal is a wait, retried once the predecessor lands — and it holds whatever `리뷰 정책` and `## 룰 설정` say. The dispatch-time landing check reads `선행` alone, as before.

**A predecessor an earlier run of the same document landed is brought in, not refused.** A frozen document is often implemented a slice per run, and every run keeps a ledger of its own, so the run planning a slice has never seen the predecessor the previous run merged. On `act --kind segment --from-declaration`, after the whole-declaration checks pass and before the planned row is written, the gate takes each token of the slice's `선행` and `머지 순서` that this ledger does not know and looks for it in the other run ledgers of the same directory — files named `<run-id>.md` whose `run` row's `설계 문서` is this run's document key; `.plan`, `.kickoff` and other companions are not read. It reads each such ledger's **last** `segment` row for the id, keeps one only when its `상태` is `머지됨` or `완료` and it carries a hex `머지 커밋`, and of several prefers the run whose `run` row's `시작` is latest (file mtime is not used: it moves on every append). **A ledger qualifies only when the slice was planned there under the declaration the frozen document carries now**, for a predecessor and for the slice being planned alike: the last `계획됨` row for the id in that ledger must equal the slice's current `선언 파일`, `레포`, `선행`, `절단점` and `리뷰 정책` (as `선언 파일 집합` on the row; backticks and outer whitespace do not count), and its `머지 순서` when either side names a slice there. Both writers of the planned row — the gate's `--from-declaration` and the driver's `plan_from_declaration` — put **`선언 다이제스트`** on it: the sha256 of those five fields of the whole declaration, normalized that way, one `키=값` line each in that order, an undeclared field as empty, and a sixth line `머지 순서=<value>` only when the slice's `머지 순서` names a slice — so a declaration without one keeps the digest it had before the field existed. A row carrying it is compared by that digest alone. **The terminal row comes first**: a `머지됨` or `완료` row carries the `선언 다이제스트` of the declaration the slice landed under — the gate's segment arm and the driver stamp it when they write the landing, from the frozen document as it stands then — and when it does, that digest decides. A reconvergence inside the run can widen the declaration after the planned row was written, and the run then reviews and merges the wider slice, so the planned row alone would refuse a landing that holds. When the terminal digest differs from the current one and equals the planned row's, the planned row is still compared field by field so the warning can name the field. A row without it is compared field by field, and a field the row clipped — `<clip> (전체: <path>)`, or `(전체: <path>)` alone — is compared by the whole value read back from that file, **never by the clip**: two lists that differ only past the clip clip to the same bytes, so a declaration grown at its end would otherwise compare equal. When that file is gone or unreadable, the ledger is no evidence. A ledger that brought the slice in and holds no such row is read through its terminal row's `출처` once — `<출처>.md` in the same directory, when its `run` row names the same document — and a slice with no `계획됨` row in either is no evidence. **A terminal row whose `출처` is `매니페스트` is its own evidence**: no run planned that slice — the row is a person's `선행 착지` claim the gate checked against the base branch when it wrote it — so there is no ledger to follow. It is compared by the `선언 다이제스트` the row itself carries, and a row without one rests on the claim and the landing alone; either way its merge commit meets the landing verdict below like any other. The row brought in from it keeps that `선언 다이제스트`, so the next run in a chain compares the same value. A ledger that differs or has no evidence drops out of the candidates with one warning naming the field, so the next candidate may still answer; the whole-document digest is not compared, because any edit would then make every slice be done again. That merge commit is then checked against the base branch of the predecessor's own target with the three-way landing verdict a deferred review obligation closes on. **Only `착지` brings it in**: the gate appends one terminal `segment` row carrying `id`, the earlier `상태`, `PR` when present, `머지 커밋`, the earlier `워크트리`, the earlier `선행` (or `없음`) and **`출처=<that run's id>`**. `미착지` and `판정 불가` refuse the plan with exit 3 and write nothing, and so does a `머지됨` whose apply this run's declaration still owes, because condition 1 would read that row as an ending and the apply would be skipped. A `plan` writes nothing and warns what the `act` would bring in. The row is written once — a later act finds the id on the ledger. It is terminal, so the cone, condition 1, the CI poller and the dispatch-time landing check all treat it as finished work, and no review obligation is issued from it. **A predecessor no run ledger records is brought in from the manifest's `선행 착지` row**, on the same act and at the same point, when no earlier run of the document landed it or its slice's `레포` is not a declared target. The row's `원격 슬러그` must equal the slice's declared `레포`, or the plan is refused with exit 3. A `레포` that is a declared target is checked with the same three-way landing verdict against that target's base branch, and a slice whose apply this run's declaration owes is refused, because the row states a merge and nothing about an apply. A `레포` that is not a target has no anchor on this host, so the verdict comes from the remote: its default branch and its own comparison of the commit against that branch (`ahead` or `identical` is `착지`, `behind` or `diverged` is `미착지`, and anything unanswered is `판정 불가`). Only `착지` brings it in, as one terminal `segment` row carrying `id`, `상태=머지됨`, the row's `머지 커밋`, `워크트리=-`, the slice's declared `선행` (or `없음`), **`출처=매니페스트`** and the `선언 다이제스트` of the slice's declaration in this run's frozen document. A token that none of the three — this ledger, an earlier run of the document, a `선행 착지` row — shows landed still meets the floor above, whose refusal names all three. **`출처` is the gate's**: a caller's `출처=` on any `segment` row is refused with exit 2, and a router does not hand-write a predecessor row in place of this import.

**A slice declared with `절단점: PR` lands as a pull request, and that terminal row qualifies too.** Besides a `머지됨`/`완료` row with a hex `머지 커밋`, an earlier run's last row for the id qualifies when it is `완료` with a numeric `PR` and a hex `커밋` — **and only when the frozen document declares that slice's `절단점` as `PR`**; the row's own value is the run's account, and a merge-cutpoint slice that stopped at an open PR has not delivered. That claim is checked against the live pull request instead of a base branch: the gate reads its `state`, `headRefOid`, `mergeCommit`, `headRefName` and `baseRefName` from the slice's target remote in one `gh pr view`, and what it requires depends on the state. An `OPEN` request — **accepted for the slice being planned itself, never for a predecessor** — comes in only when its head equals the row's `커밋`. A `MERGED` request is not compared by head, because a person finishing it (a review fix pushed, the branch updated) moves the head and the merge is a person's act already; it comes in when the same answer carries all three of: a merge commit on the target's base branch, a `headRefName` equal to the row's `브랜치` where that name has the slice's segment-branch shape `seg/<슬러그>-<세그먼트>`, and a `baseRefName` equal to the target's base branch. The two branch names take over the head comparison's other job, which was tying the row's `PR` to this slice. Not doing a slice again is not building on it: every segment branches from the base, and an open request's work is on no base, so an open request keeps its own slice from being dispatched again and satisfies no dependent. A request that is closed, cannot be read, is open with a head that has moved, is open where a predecessor is asked for, or is merged without all three of those holds brings nothing in, without refusing — a row with no `브랜치`, or one not shaped as the slice's segment branch, cannot be held to a merged request and so never brings one in. The row brought in carries `커밋`, the merge commit as `머지 커밋` when the request was merged, and the earlier row's `브랜치` when it had one, so the next run in a chain can hold the request to the branch again; `출처` is the earlier row's own `출처` when it had one, and otherwise that run's id. The dispatch floor does not take a brought-in `완료` without a hex `머지 커밋` as a landed predecessor.

**The slice being planned is brought in the same way.** After its predecessors, the same act looks for the planned slice itself, when this ledger does not know it, in the earlier ledgers by the rules above. When it qualifies and its landing holds, the gate appends its terminal row and **writes no planned row** — the act ends with 0, and a `plan` says so on its `통과 예상` line. For the slice itself a landing that does not hold — `미착지`, `판정 불가`, a merge whose apply this run still owes, a pull request that no longer stands — **is not a refusal**: nothing is brought in, a warning names the reason, and the slice is planned and done again. Skipping work that never landed is the harm there; redoing it is the remedy.

**The fixed-graph driver uses the same import.** `plan_from_declaration` calls the gate's import function for every declared slice, through `run_gate_call`, before it writes the planned rows; the function derives the document from the manifest itself, because that child shell carries none. A slice brought in goes back into `done.txt`, gets **no** `계획됨` row — a planned row behind the terminal one would be the last row the next run's import reads — and is not dispatched by the main loop; the morning report counts it on its own line. A slice brought in at a request still open goes to `skipped.txt` instead: the main loop does not dispatch it either and reports it as `완성-미착지`, but `deps_satisfied` reads `done.txt` alone, so a slice that depends on it parks as it does in the run that left the request open. A failed call plans the slice. When a slice declared with `절단점: PR` returns `완성-미착지` from the deadline check or from the merge gate, the driver observes the branch's pull request and, when it is open or merged, writes the terminal row `상태=완료 · PR · 커밋=<head> · 브랜치 · 레포 · 워크트리` that a later run imports; a slice declared with a merge cutpoint, a plan with no declaration, an apply refusal and an unobservable request write none.

**`blocked.스코프=cone` is the one scope the router may CREATE, and the polarity is the opposite of run scope's.** A run-scope block is raised by the gate and only resolved by the router; a cone holds what stands on a refuted premise and lets the siblings keep going. The gate does not take the router's `의존 세그먼트` on trust — it derives the cone itself and refuses a declaration that is a proper subset. Widening passes; narrowing does not.

**`blocked.원인=판정 불가` records an ancestry probe that could not be answered, and its disposition is fail-closed.** `git merge-base --is-ancestor` distinguishes three outcomes, and only exit 1 means "not an ancestor"; 128 means an object is not there, and reading it as "not an ancestor" would fail open. So the segment **stays in the cone** and the row says what could not be measured.

**`종료 절.상태=보류` is not `불가능` with a softer name.** Impossible ends the clause forever; on hold says a person's answer is outstanding and a successor run picks it up. Its `근거` must therefore name an **open** approval whose cutpoint is the literal `판단`, and the gate confirms that id exists in the ledger with `상태=대기` — evidence rather than wording, on the same terms every other clause settlement takes.

**`승인.절단점=판단` is written by the gate.** The router never chooses to ask: it submits its own recommendation through `act --kind judgment`, and whether that becomes a question is decided here. A grade-2 judgment is raised to one, and so is a grade-1 judgment that does not clear the auto-adoption floor. The approval's id is derived from the judgment rather than from an argv, so the same judgment submitted twice yields one approval instead of a queue, and its **binding tuple binds the question and the menu rather than a tree**, because a question's answer is an input to work that has not started and has no tree to measure (the four-component serialization stated under §3.2).

**The resubmission is the consumption of the answer.** The gate reads the approval's state before issuing anything: `승인` adopts the judgment and writes `자율 승인` carrying `해소 승인=<id>`; `거부` and `무효` refuse and do not re-ask; `대기` returns quietly. **A held-class approval's (`사람-결정` or `시각-승인`) `거부` or `무효` is consumed instead of refused**: the router writes the segment's `park` row and resubmits the judgment, and that resubmission writes `자율 승인` with `결정=거절 소비` and `해소 승인=<id>` and exits 0 — the row is what takes the refusal out of `answered_judgments[]`. Before the `park` row it is refused with exit 3, and until it is consumed the refusal holds its segment as an open question does (stage dispatch, `replan` and re-plan clause 7). A `사람-결정` answered in free text closes `상태=승인` with `처분 사유=자유 입력`, and the resubmission that consumes it writes `결정=자유 입력 소비` rather than `채택`. A consuming resubmission may cite the answered id as `답한 승인=<id>` instead of repeating `근거`: the gate takes that id once it is a `사람-결정` approval on the same segment, answered and not yet consumed, so the row's `근거` can say what the router did. A second resubmission finds the id named and is refused like any other closed question. Issuing a held-class question needs a `--segment` that has a `segment` row, and a segment gets at most two distinct ones per class (a `시각-승인` resubmitted in the same words is one id and never reaches the cap); the third is refused with exit 3 and writes nothing. **A closed approval is never re-opened by a resubmission** — the id derivation guarantees only a genuinely different question reaches a new id — and **one answer opens one judgment**, enforced by refusing any id a `자율 승인` row already names.

**That durability is also why termination condition 2 excludes it.** An act approval's window closes with the night, so a run may not end while one is open; a question's answer survives into a successor run. What records the residual instead is a third class in the `done` file, `종단 — 질의 잔여 N건 · 승인 <id>…`, sitting beside `무효화` for the same structural reason. **No eleventh termination condition is created**: a condition exists to *refuse* a proposal, and an open question must not refuse one. This sentence rules out making an open question into a condition; it does not fix the number of conditions, which is `gate_done_conditions`' own and moves when a condition is added for a reason of its own — condition 11 refuses a proposal while the `리뷰-후-머지` rule is not set to `끔` and, for a segment under `선리뷰후머지`, the last `segment` row that carries `머지 대조` reads `위반(…)`, `리뷰 없음` or `판정 불가`. The condition names its passing values — `충족`, `정책 면제`, `이월` and `기각` — and holds on every other value, an empty one included: `이월` records a merge the merge rule let through after the last cycle's open P1 findings were carried over to issues, and `기각` a merge of a last cycle a person dismissed at the seat, which the merge rule and the `다음` table read before the counts. A count violation clears only through that dismissal and a second `머지됨` row; a run cannot write a dismissal, so an unattended run stays held until a person answers.

**`산출물 없는 정지` is the sixth terminal class, so a stage that correctly refused to decide for the user is not punished like one that produced nothing.**

Its definition is the four-part conjunction: **exit code 0, artifact predicate false, no halt record, and a trace in that stage's ndjson of reaching a decision point the skill directed it to.** The last clause is what separates it from `공허한 성공` — not "attempted nothing" but "arrived and did not decide". Its disposition is not a retry: the point is promoted to a pending approval, and that promotion is the recorded skip the unattended `design` step-6 default calls for.

**The other branch is invisible.** A stage that improvises and produces output lands as `정상 완료`, since the audit and review predicates are forgeable, so the morning report's heading is `기록된 자율 결정` rather than "every autonomous decision".

**One control partially recovers it.** In Mode A the router owns the stage's ndjson, so a `ToolSearch` naming `AskUserQuestion` with no halt record is a high-precision signal that the stage improvised. An appearance naming `mcp__cc-cmds__question_form` counts as the same signal: the detector matches either name.

**Every park names a scope and a cause, and one that cannot is a bug rather than
a decision.** `act` means a terminal act is blocked and NOTHING else stops — the
segment's artifacts survive and it is reported as 완성-미착지. `cone` means a
premise downstream work stands on has been refuted, so what depends on it stops
and its siblings do not. `run` means the run cannot judge the state a later
irreversible act would transform, or its own anchor is invalid; it stops the
declared blast radius. The anti-rule these exist to enforce: **a blocked act
never escalates to a cone or a run stop, and the absence of an answer is not a
cause**, so one cutpoint typo does not park every segment.

**`사이클 예산 소진` is separate from `사다리 R4` because they are different
events**; filing the first under the second makes the ledger say the ladder ran
out when it never started. `사다리 단 부재` is a third thing again — a transition
into a rung this run's authorization does not make available. It is a park and
not a clamp: clamping the rung would disarm the ladder's only end. `리뷰 잔여`
is a fourth: the disposition of `다음=잔여 보류` once the re-checks pass
`RECHECK_ROUND_CAP`, or of a carry-over that failed after a re-check,
a `스코프=cone` park (`원인=막힘` from the routing shift, whose cone recorder
takes no other cause; `원인=무효화` from the driver, like its other cone
parks) that carries the one issue it filed in `이슈`
and the residual class (`P0 잔존` \| `분류 미상` \| `차단 P1` \| `이월 실패`) at
the start of `근거`, and no `재킥 원인`. It is a finished disposition with its
findings on record, whereas `사이클 예산 소진` is now only a defensive park — a
review loop that left without a value that ends it.

**`적용 불명` is its own termination class and not a kind of crash.** A crash is
an execution that did not complete; this one completed and left a state nobody
can describe. `적용 준비` is likewise separate from `실행중`: it names a worktree
pinned to a merge commit, which is the artifact a person needs when an apply's
outcome is unknown. A run cut off while its supervisor ran leaves `적용 준비`
last; that is not a terminal state, and a settlement (`act --kind
apply-settle`) measures it, or a person settles it at the `적용 정산` site.

**Severity adjudication and parking are not new kinds.** A severity tie-break is a `자율 승인` row with `kind=severity`; a park is a `blocked` row with a `사유`.

**An approval and a review obligation are new kinds, because their rows advance through states** — issued, waiting, resolved or stale — and the reader of an existing kind takes every row as terminal.

**`되돌리는 법` is the field that makes the report readable rather than merely complete**: the unimportant decisions are bearable only when they are cheap to undo in the morning. So the field carries a concrete command or edit, not a claim, and failing to produce one is itself the escalation trigger.

**`등급` and `기준` come from the judgment-grade contract** (`_common/judgment-grade.md`). `기준` names the authored standard that chose the option, one a later reader can check. Grade 0 writes no row at all: a rule that fully determines the answer produced no decision to record.

**`kind=severity` requires `finding-id`.** The review rule defaults to the higher severity *unless the lead resolved the dispute*, so the exception counts as fired **only** where a `자율 승인` row records the decision, the rejected alternative, both rationales, and the finding it applies to; with no such row the rule's default branch applies. The interactive path is unchanged.

### 3.4 Write form and its diff gate

One write form: **append** — a new row, or a new `## 실행 <run-id>` block. Gate: **0 removed lines**. `segment.상태` advancing is expressed as a **new `segment` row** for the same `id`, not as an edit; readers take the last row for an `id` as current. This keeps a single append gate for the whole schema and leaves the run's history intact for the morning audit.

### 3.4a The chain, and why it is not only about forgery

Every row carries `prev=<64 hex>` — the sha256 of the preceding row's bytes. The first row of a block chains to the block heading.

It makes a rewrite visible, and it also catches §3.1a's interleaving, which **no row-grammar check can see** — the line count stays right and the field values splice — so one mechanism detects both, and deletion and reordering with them. A spliced row can satisfy a termination condition that the true rows do not, so corruption here **manufactures a false completion**; the advisory lock keeps interleaving rare and the chain makes the rare case detectable.

**Single writer is a property of components, not of processes.** Each gate invocation is a separate shell, so two acts in flight are two writers against one file; the lock is mandatory rather than defensive.

### 3.5 The morning report is a ledger-referenced companion

The report lives at `<base>/docs/pipeline-run/{runId}.md` — the same `<kind>` directory and the same kind token, so **no new kind is created** and the §1.2 guards apply to it unchanged. It is named by `<run-id>` rather than by `{slug}`, which means it is **not independently re-derivable** from the design document the way §1.1 sidecars are; it is found through the `보고서` field of the `run` row. Run-id naming is admissible here and not for the ledger because only the ledger must be findable with nothing but the document in hand.

- **Writer**: the driver. **Created as a stub by `autopilot` at kickoff**, so the file exists even if the run dies mid-way.
- **It must be durable independently of any banner**, because the notification seat's contract does not include delivery confirmation. The report is the source of truth; the banner is a courtesy. Every event writes the report **first** and attempts the banner second — the immediate-notification events included.
- **Two seats raise banners and neither is an agent — the liveness watcher and the adjudication gate.** The prohibition is on a *spawned stage* deciding whether a banner reaches the user, and neither seat is spawned. What is still not promised is **delivery**: this notifier cannot override a focus mode, so **the run may not reach a sleeping user at all**, and the morning report is the whole of the guarantee.
- **It enumerates every autonomous decision the run RECORDED** — the heading is `기록된 자율 결정`, because a stage that improvises and produces output writes no row — all `자율 승인` rows grouped by `kind` with decision, rejected alternative and rationale carried verbatim; every fix the ladder auto-adopted; every parked item with its `사유`; and each stage's `종단 부류`.
- **It also enumerates every `계측 필링 건너뜀` row** with its `사유` and `트리거`, beside the `자율 승인` rows with `kind=metrics-filing` — the ninth item of the rendering enumeration in `autopilot`'s morning report. Where there is none, the report says so: an absent row is "nothing was due or everything was filed", never "the collector found nothing", and the report has to keep those apart. The selector is the `kind` and not `절단점=필링`, because the halt-report rows below carry that cutpoint too.
- **It also enumerates the halt reports**, the item right after it: every `자율 승인` row with `kind=halt-report` (`결정`, `런`, `서명`, `이슈`, `담기`, and for `담기=실패` the command file `<ledger directory>/halt-report.unfiled/<이슈>.md`) and every `중단 리포트 건너뜀` row with its `사유`, `런` and `서명` — rows that usually describe other runs, since they land in the ledger of whichever run hosted the round — and this run's own disposition, read from the `런 처분` field of its record `<state root>/cc-cmds/halt-report/runs/<run-id>.json`, or `기록 없음` when there is no record. A report written before the run settled reads `정착 대기`, which is the ordinary first reading and not a failure.
- **A run with a `설계 티어` row gets a 「설계 티어 대조」 part in Act 3**, after the outcome summary: the judged and confirmed tier, whether an audit ran, the estimated and the declared file count, the rationale and the heavy reasons, and per segment the first review's P0/P1, the cumulative P0 and the dismissals with their P0. `autopilot`'s morning report renders it; the driver's `report_append` carries no such line. A run without the row — one whose plan predates the tier keys — has no such part.

**The limit is stated with the control.** The report is itself authored by the run, so it is powerless against a run that improvises a decision **and also** omits it from its own report. It is not a gate, and **the real protection against an irreversible autonomous act remains the permission cutpoint of §2.3.**

### 3.6 The metrics summary beside the ledger

The run metrics collector (`orchestrator/collect-run-metrics.sh`) is started by the gate from its prelude — every verb but `plan` and `digest-path`, never from a stage seat, at most once per six hours per state root (stamp `<state root>/metrics.stamp`, one-attempt lock `<state root>/.metrics.lock` with a two-hour expiry). The verb that meets the expired stamp pays only for the stamp, the lock and the launch: the round runs in a detached child (`gate.sh metrics-round`, bound to the lock by a nonce written into it), which cuts the collector after one hour, files, and releases the lock; its output goes to `<RUN_DIR>/log/metrics-round.log`. It is the only writer of the six files below; the gate reads its stdout and nothing else.

| File | Where | What |
| --- | --- | --- |
| summary | `<base>/docs/pipeline-run/metrics.json` | the aggregate over every collected run, keys sorted, **no timestamps** |
| start marker | `<base>/docs/pipeline-run/metrics.json.pending` | created empty when a round starts, removed after the summary is renamed into place; one left behind is a round that died |
| per-run record | `<base>/docs/pipeline-run/metrics/<run-id>.json` | one per collected run, written once and not rewritten |
| per-run member record | `<base>/docs/pipeline-run/metrics/<run-id>.members.json` | the team members' dispatch spans of one run, written beside the per-run record only when that run is newly collected; it is not a per-run record — the summary's strata and the triggers never read it, and only the summary's `members` key is aggregated from these files |
| unfiled | `<base>/docs/pipeline-run/metrics.unfiled/<issue number>.md` | an issue that was created but could not be added to the Project, with the command that adds it |
| round journal | `<state root>/metrics/<repo-key>/rounds.jsonl` | append-only, one line per round; `repo-key` is the repo base's absolute path with `/` turned into `-` |

**The summary carries no timestamps so that the same input gives the same bytes.** Two rounds over the same runs then leave the file byte-identical, and a changed summary always means changed input. **Round numbers, consecutive counts and times live only in the journal**, and the journal is the only source the trend trigger reads for its baseline and its run of bad or good rounds — a counter kept anywhere else could disagree with the lines it summarises. The stamp, the lock and the journal sit beside the reaper's root but are not reaped: the reaper walks `run/` only.

The population is every ledger in the directory whose run is `종단` or `버려짐` by the same state function the snapshot uses; a round with a run in that population that could not be collected judges nothing.

**An account switch is read from `stage-result.계정` and from nothing else.** A row is a switch when its `계정` names a real account and the previous row of the same segment that named a real account named a different one; an absent field, `-` and `(미상)` name no account and stand on neither side of the comparison. The excluded span opens at that stage's own first transcript timestamp and runs for the switch window (`switch_window_s`, 900 seconds by default); requests and compaction records inside it leave both terms of the net effect, and the part of it that overlaps the stage's transcript leaves the time term's denominator too, while the stratum's `A5` wall clock keeps it. **`교대 기동.사유=상한` is not an account event**: it marks the kickoff shift and a shift that reached the context hard cap, and the collector does not read it for switches. The summary prints the number of switches it found as `switch_events`.

**The filing settings file is `~/.config/cc-cmds/metrics-filing`** (`${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/cc-cmds/metrics-filing`). Lines are `key<TAB>value`, read with `awk` and never sourced, and two keys are recognised:

- `project` — the number of the observation-only GitHub Project that instrument issues are added to. Anything but digits reads as absent. **While it is absent no issue is created at all** and the round writes `계측 필링 건너뜀 | 사유=번호 없음` instead.
- `account` — the GitHub login the write-scoped credential must resolve to. A credential that resolves to anything else is `사유=조회 실패`, not a filing under the wrong name. **That login needs triage access or more on `Nharu/cc-cmds`**, because the gate creates the label before each issue; without it every round that fires ends as `사유=조회 실패`.

The file holds no credential; the token comes from `credentials.sh` as it does for every other write.

**Instrument issues go to `Nharu/cc-cmds`, whichever run's ledger hosted the round.** Every issue and label call names that repository with `--repo` and runs with `GH_REPO`, `GH_HOST`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` unset, so neither the hosting run's checkout and remotes nor an inherited variable can move the destination. **That repository is public, and the body leaves the hosting repository for it**: the body never carries the hosting checkout's name or an absolute local path, only the base's repo tag and the summary's path inside the base. **The `cc-metrics` label is not a prerequisite**: before each issue creation the gate creates it with `gh label create --force` under the write-scoped credential, because gh resolves `--label` before it creates anything and a missing label fails the whole creation. A failure of that call, like any other failed GitHub call on this path, is `사유=조회 실패` and no issue is created.

**Every base's issues share that one tracker, so each issue is namespaced by the base's repo tag.** The tag is the first 12 hex digits of the sha256 of the base's `repo-key` (its absolute path with `/` turned into `-`, as in the table above), computed by the gate from the base of the ledger that hosted the round. It keeps the checkout's name from a reader and is not a secret — anyone who guesses the path can confirm it by hashing. A title is `[cc-metrics <tag>] <signature>`. The round reads the open `cc-metrics` issues and keeps only those whose title starts with its own base's `[cc-metrics <tag>] `; the open-issue cap (`사유=상한 도달`), the comment on an open `T6/*` issue and the close all act on that filtered list alone. An issue with another base's tag, or an untagged `[cc-metrics] ` title, is neither counted, commented on nor closed.

**Instrument issues are an exception to the repository's autopilot tracking rule, and the exception is deliberate.** Issues labelled `cc-metrics` are added to the observation-only Project named by `project` and **not** to the autopilot Project the repository instructions say every autopilot defect goes to. They are measurements of the compaction window and of the collector itself, filed by a machine at most once per round; putting them on the work queue would bury the human-filed items that queue exists for. A later session must not "fix" this by moving them onto the autopilot Project.

---

## 3b. `cc-pipeline-approval v1` — the approval sidecar

```
# 파이프라인 승인 기록 — <run-id>
<!-- cc-pipeline-approval v1; writer=gate; reader=gate; owner-run=<run-id>; owner-doc=<document key> | (없음); NOT a design doc; mechanism-local, never staged by a skill -->
```

**Path**: `<base>/docs/pipeline-approval/<run-id>.md`. Keyed on the RUN rather than derived from the design document by `sidecar.md` §1.1's slug rule, because a run may start from a pull request or a bare intent and have no document to derive from at all. This is therefore a kind under §1.2's exception clause, and this section states everything that clause requires of such a kind.

**Header and proof pair.** Where a document-keyed kind carries `owner-doc=`, this one carries `owner-run=<run-id>` and, beside it, `owner-doc=` for the record (`(없음)` when the run names no document). Its proof pair is `owner-run=<run-id>` **together with the existence of that run's authorization record** `<base>/docs/pipeline-grant/<run-id>.md`. A reader takes the file as this run's only when both halves hold, and a writer refuses to create or extend it otherwise — fail-closed in both directions.

**Writer and directory.** The gate is the only writer, and — §1.1 makes directory creation the writer's duty — **the gate creates `<base>/docs/pipeline-approval/` immediately before its first write**, so the first approval of a fresh checkout does not swallow a free-input answer.

**Blocks.** One block per approval, `## 승인 <승인 id>`, holding two fenced regions:

````
## 승인 J-867db2b3
**질문 sha256**: <64 hex>
```text
<the full question text>
```
**답변 sha256**: <64 hex>
```text
<the full answer text>
```
````

The **immutable region** — `질문 sha256` and its fence — is written when the approval is issued and never rewritten. The **mutable region** — `답변 sha256` and its fence — is filled when the approval closes, and re-filled if a later answer supersedes an earlier one; a free-input answer (ledger `처분 사유=자유 입력`) is written here and nowhere else — except on a `사람-결정` approval, whose free input closes it and is copied to the answer store `answer/<id>.md` as well. Fences are one backtick longer than the longest backtick run in the payload, never shorter than three, so a payload cannot close its own fence; the tokenizer matches a closing fence by the exact backtick string that opened it and treats a `## 승인` line inside an open fence as payload. Act and boundary approvals get a block too — their question is a fixed literal, but the row's anchor has to name something and their closing rows carry the answer digest.

**Write form.** §1.3 of `sidecar.md` unchanged: a temp file in the same directory, a compare-and-swap against the bytes the build read, a plain `mv`, a read-back that the block heading is present, a bounded retry. Nothing here deletes or truncates.

**What the ledger carries about it.** Every `승인` row that this kind backs carries `사이드카 앵커=<run-id>#<승인 id>`, and the row's digests — the question digest inside `구속 튜플`, `답변 다이제스트` on a closing row — are digests OF the fenced texts in that block. `응답 토큰` names the transcript frame, not the sidecar, and does not stand in for the anchor.

---

## 4. `cc-pipeline-halt v1` — the halt record (not a sidecar)

When an unattended arm reaches a point that needs a human, it writes a halt record and stops. This is the **durable form of the plain-text failure report that `implement`'s fail-loud rule already prescribes** — a proper subset of it — so no shipped fail-loud paragraph changes.

```
${RUN_DIR}/halt/<stage-id>.md
RUN_DIR = ${XDG_STATE_HOME:-$HOME/.local/state}/cc-cmds/run/<run-id>
```

```
<!-- cc-pipeline-halt v1; writer=<skill>; reader=orchestrator; stage=<stage-id>; run=<run-id> -->
**중단 시각**: <ISO8601>
**스킬**: <skill name>
**스텝**: <step identifier>
**분류**: tool-unavailable | gate-unanswerable | freeze-mismatch | precondition-failed
**질문 문면**: <the Korean question that would have been asked, verbatim, never summarized>
**선택지**:
- `<label>` — <description, verbatim>
**추천**: `<label>` — <why this option, one line> | (없음) — <the information only a person has>
**추천 근거**: <종류>:<quote> / <종류>:<quote>
**잔여 판정**: <부류>: R<n>, R<n> / <부류>: R<n>
**하네스 오류**: <the original harness error string, verbatim> | (없음)
**관측 상세**: <the measurement values this 분류 owes, parts joined with ` / `> | (없음)
**재호출 명령**: <the command line the skill would have emitted, verbatim> | (없음)
**후속**: 보류 큐
<!-- /cc-pipeline-halt v1 -->
```

- **A routing shift's record is written by the gate, one level down.** `act --kind shift-halt` (§3.2) writes `${RUN_DIR}/halt/shift/<segment>#<n>.md`, `<n>` one more than the segment's earlier shift-halt rows, with `writer=gate`, `stage=<segment>#<n>`, `**스킬**: autopilot-router-shift`, `**스텝**: 교대 정지 — <자리>`, `**분류**: gate-unanswerable`, the two options of the site table, `**추천**` and `**추천 근거**` as checked there, and `(없음)` in `하네스 오류` and `재호출 명령`. It is a shift's record only through the gate's cone row naming it with its digest — never by its header — and it is never resumed: the next shift carries out the answered option itself.
- **An implement stage's enforcement-surface edit is a third halt form, the seat apply.** A stage cannot commit a file the enforcement-surface classifier (`hooks/enforcement-surfaces.sh`) puts in `규칙카탈로그` or `지침파일`, so `implement-unattended` writes the edit into a record and the gate commits it once a person approves. `**분류**: gate-unanswerable`; `**스텝**` begins with the literal `Step 3 강제 표면 좌석 적용` (free text may follow after ` — `); and four fields sit after `**질문 문면**` and before `**선택지**`:

  ```
  **좌석 적용 기준**: <the segment HEAD after the stage's last commit, 40 hex>
  **좌석 적용 파일**:
  - 파일 `<repo-relative path>` — <class token> — 기준 <40-hex blob|없음> — 모드 <100644|100755|삭제> — <the whole new content as one base64 line|삭제>
  **사람 몫 파일**:
  - 파일 `<repo-relative path>` — <class token> — <one-line reason>
  **좌석 적용 커밋 제목**: <type(scope): one Korean subject line>
  ```

  - **The file lines begin with `- 파일 `, never with ``- ` ``,** because ``- ` `` at a line head is the option line's shape and several readers scan the whole record for it; a file line in that shape would be read as a label.
  - **`**사람 몫 파일**` is optional** and holds the classified files the run never commits (`프로젝트설정`, `트랜스크립트`, and any path the checks below would refuse). A `지침파일` the base does not track is not one of them and is in neither list: the base's `CLAUDE.md` route (`gate.sh exec`) decides it, and that route refuses a stage's copy with exit 3, so such an edit stops at the gate's refusal and does not finish inside the run. A record with only such files has no `**좌석 적용 기준**`, `**좌석 적용 파일**` or `**좌석 적용 커밋 제목**`.
  - **The options are fixed.** A record that applies offers `좌석 적용` — the gate commits the files above on the base commit as they are when a person approves, the seat re-attaches this session, and the approval also covers later enforcement-surface edits in the same segment inside the scope — and `보류` — nothing is applied and the segment goes to the hold queue. A record with only person's files offers `사람 몫으로 넘김` — the run continues without the edit, a person makes it outside the run, and it stays in the morning report and the PR body — and `보류`.
  - **It carries no `**추천**` and no `**추천 근거**`:** the gate refuses this step's recommendation structurally, as it refuses the two `좌석 실행` steps', and no shift exception, adopted copy or automatic class takes it.
  - **The gate checks every file line before it writes,** and refuses the whole record when one fails: a repo-relative path without `..`, a leading `/` or `//`; a path the classifier puts in a class the seat may apply; inside the segment's declared file set; not ignored in the segment worktree; a `지침파일` the base tracks; the base blob as recorded (`없음` when the base lacks the path); a mode of the three, `삭제` only for a path the base tracks; and no symbolic link at the path or any of its ancestors. It reads the record once into a private copy, takes the worktree from the ledger's segment row, needs the tree clean with HEAD at `**좌석 적용 기준**`, builds the commit in a temporary index without touching the worktree, fast-forwards to it, and resets the branch to the base when the row cannot be written. `gate.sh seat-apply-preview --manifest … --record <path> --target <alias>` runs the same decoding and the same tree building in an isolated object directory, writes nothing, and prints the combined diff, the `--numstat`, the record digest, the scope and the question line the seat asks.
  - **How the answer is taken is in the `중단 답` paragraph of §3.2:** a person's `좌석 적용` needs a proven question frame, and a seat's `좌석 적용` needs an earlier person's answer in the same segment whose scope covers the record.
  - **The gate checks where each classified path on the branch came from**, at the review dispatch and the merge act, and the driver reads the same check before it merges (`gate.sh surface-provenance --manifest … --segment <id>`). For every path the classifier classes that changed between the merge base and HEAD, the HEAD tree entry (mode and object id, absent counting as a value) must equal the base end's, or the entry the last seat-apply commit of this run and segment that touched the path left. A path changed on both the base and the segment, a path changed with no seat-apply commit, or one that differs from its last seat-apply commit is refused: the review dispatch is held with a `blocked` row reading `사유=강제 표면이 좌석 적용 밖에서 바뀜`, and the merge act exits 3. A branch that shares no history with the base end is compared from the empty tree, so every classified path it carries is one it added: one it holds as the base end does passes, and one the base end holds differently is refused as changed on both sides. A check that cannot be decided — the classifier, the merge base or the changed paths cannot be read, or the repository is shallow, where a missing merge base proves no orphan — exits 2 from `surface-provenance` and still refuses the review dispatch and the merge act, under its own reason: the dispatch writes one `blocked` row reading `원인=판정 불가` and `사유=강제 표면 출처 판정 불가`, the driver parks under `강제 표면 출처 판정 불가`, and the cone seed takes only the first kind, leaving an unreadable segment to the ancestry axis's own `판정 불가` row. The cone a `snapshot` derives for its `shift_halts` seeds the same way and writes no row. A stage's `exec` whose argument the classifier classes is refused too, naming the seat-apply step; a `지침파일` path the segment worktree's HEAD does not track is left out of that refusal and meets the base's `CLAUDE.md` rules instead, which refuse a stage's copy.
- **A visual publish stage's record names its site in `**스텝**`.** `visual-publish-unattended` (the `S2P` step) writes four sites, each with a fixed `**분류**`: `전제-불일치` and `발행-실패` read `precondition-failed`, `시각-판정` and `금지-후보` read `gate-unanswerable`. `**스텝**` is the site itself or `<site> — <free text>`, the two forms the gate matches a site in. `시각-판정` (`승인` · `수정 요청` · `거부`), `금지-후보` (`승인` · `거절` · `나중에`) and `발행-실패` (`다시 발행` · `발행하지 않음`) take a person's answer only (`중단 답`, §3.2), carry `**추천**: (없음) — …`, and stand in the gate's re-entry table, so a fresh session handed the card enters the answer's branch without publishing again; `전제-불일치` offers no option. A `금지-후보` record carries one line more after `**질문 문면**`, `**금지 후보**: <candidate id>` — the id the gate compares with the line the stage passes to `visual-config.sh append` after a person's `승인`, which that one answer opens once.
- **Each option is its own line, `- \`<label>\` — <description>`, under `**선택지**:`.** The seat records a person's answer only as one of these lines, matched byte for byte, so options run inline after the colon — `\`a\` — … / \`b\` — …`, the form a reference may use to name them in prose — are options nobody can answer: the gate refuses every one of them, and the halt stops the run however the person answers. **The option block runs from the `**선택지**:` line to the next line that begins with `**` or the closing fence,** and only the lines inside it are options: an option-shaped line elsewhere in the record — a command line of a command halt, a line quoted into the question, a line after a `**` field that ended the block, or one below a closing fence written too early — is not one, and the gate refuses an answer that names it.
- **`**추천**` and `**추천 근거**` are optional, and they are what lets the seat answer without a person.** A record with options carries both, after the option lines and before `**하네스 오류**`; a record with no option carries neither. Neither line starts with ``- ` ``, so neither is read as an option. `**추천**` names one option's label byte for byte, or reads `(없음) — <the information only a person has>`. `**추천 근거**` lists what the choice rests on, each part `<종류>:<quote>`, `<종류>` one of `인터뷰 기록` · `매니페스트 인가` · `기기 사실` · `상시 지침`. The seat answers only with that label (`halt-answer` with `답한 주체=좌석`, §3.2), never with an option it picked itself, and the gate copies `<종류>` into the row as `근거 종류`, so the morning report still reads it after the run directory is gone. **The writers are these, and their rules are written here once:** `design-audit-unattended` (the `Step 7 / 골격급 재발` halt and each finding's halt in the reconcile pass), `design-reconverge` (the S1·S3·S4 halts), `implement-unattended` (the CFI-U3 BT-STOP and every other `gate-unanswerable` halt that offers options, except the two `좌석 실행` halts — `Step 3 잔여 검증 좌석 실행` and `Step 3 게이트 도달 좌석 실행` — which the seat runs and never answers from a recommendation, and the `Step 3 강제 표면 좌석 적용` halt, which a person answers and which carries no recommendation), `review-unattended` (the `Step 1b 빈 대상` halt, whose recommendation is `구현 재파견` resting on the `기기 사실` of the commit count), `design-discuss-unattended` (its own halt sites) and `design-solo-unattended` (its own halt sites, by the discuss arm's rules). Each writer's skill cites this bullet at its halt sites. The rules:
  - **Recommend only an option the run has a path to carry out.** An option whose answer no stage or shift of this run can execute is not recommended, whoever would pick it — for example `escalate to team round`, since no in-run route assembles a team after the first design. Recommend the option the run can carry out, and the requirement that went unmet stays in the document as an unresolved issue.
  - **Never recommend an option whose description carries `위험 수용`, `게이트 비활성`, `그대로 진행` or `범위 축소`.** Those are the risk-acceptance shapes of `judgment-grade.md` grade 2, which are never delegated; the gate refuses a seat answer whose recommended option's description carries any of them.
  - **Write `(없음)` when the four 근거 kinds do not settle the choice** — an intent or priority the records do not hold, or a fact outside the repository and this machine. Then the seat asks a person, and there is no conservative default.
  - **A design stage's `skeleton` halt follows these rules, and its `ladder-unsettled` halt the ladder's writing rules** (`design-discuss-unattended` Step 7U). Either raises a `설계-골격` judgment only when it writes `(없음)`.
  - **`design-audit-unattended`'s `Step 7 / 골격급 재발` halt always writes `(없음)`.** A re-audit that still finds a skeleton-grade defect asks whether the run may change its skeleton a second time, and only a person holds that intent.
- **`**잔여 판정**`** is carried by a `/ ladder-unsettled` record only, one line; its grammar is the ladder's writing rules', and the gate checks a seat answer to it as `중단 답` (§3.2) says.
- **Written with the atomic form of `sidecar.md` §1.3** (temp file in the same directory, then rename), so a partial record is never observed. The closing fence is the terminator: a record whose last non-empty line is not `<!-- /cc-pipeline-halt v1 -->` is **a crash mid-write, not a halt**.
- **`관측 상세` is where measurement values go, and `질문 문면` is not.** A `freeze-mismatch` halt owes which assertion diverged, the baseline value, the observed value, `FROZEN_SHA256`, and — on an assertion 1 mismatch — the intersecting paths, which `verification.md` §6.0 calls the only thing telling a later reader what ended the window. **On a 2b mismatch it owes three more**, because that assertion compares a subset of the own-entry: the diverging **component** as one of the closed three `worktree` | `branch` | `HEAD-tree`; on a `HEAD-tree` divergence the two **tree** shas as the compared pair plus the changed path count and up to five names from a tree-to-tree `git diff --name-only`; and the two `HEAD` commit shas as **reported-never-compared diagnostics**, which keep the forensic trail of an empty commit. `질문 문면` is the verbatim question a human uses to audit a forged halt, so these values never go there. It is **one line like every other field line in this block**, parts joined with ` / `, and every field line sits above the closing fence because that fence is the terminator. A class with no measurement values of its own writes `(없음)`.
- **An exit 11 from the gate is a halt input, not a retry input.** 도달 park says
  the act was not performed and will not be: the verdict is keyed on the act
  digest, so the same argv re-declared takes the recorded answer. A stage whose
  parked act was not essential continues without it and writes no halt record at
  all; one whose act was essential writes this record with `분류:
  gate-unanswerable` and the `도달 판정` value in `관측 상세`.
- **`재호출 명령` is inert.** The driver records it and **never executes it**. Auto-running it retries a condition whose cause is still present, turning a single pass into a loop bounded only by budget.
- **Termination discipline**: write the record atomically → take **no further step** (no cleanup beyond what the halting step already committed, no partial progress, no fallback act) → end the turn normally.
- **A person's answer comes back as a resume, or as an answer card to a fresh session — never as a re-run.** The seat records the answer as a `중단 답` row (§3.2) and a shift hands it on with the carry act (`act --kind skill --carry-answer <line>`). The gate asks for the halted session first; when its account has no room and the halt is one the gate's re-entry table holds — an audit's synthesis halt, a re-convergence's Step 3 halt, the visual publish stage's `시각-판정`, `금지-후보` and `발행-실패`, an implement stage's halt, a design discussion's halt, a lead solo design's halt, a base split's halt — it starts a fresh session instead, and either one reads the answer from the card `CC_PIPELINE_HALT_ANSWER` names. The last four are carried in their ordinary dispatch form, which the gate builds from the ledger and hands the shift as the `answered_halts[]` element's `carry_prompt`; the shift sends it byte for byte, and a carry of this run's answer whose ledger-valued slot differs from the built value is re-attached instead. For an answer a later run brought in (`출처 런`) the ledger is the earlier run's, where its attempt halted, and **such an answer is never re-attached**, since no session of the later run halted there: wherever an answer of this run would be re-attached — a halt the table does not hold, a scope the gate cannot check, a slot that differs, an implement stop of a base resolution, a CI fix or a re-implementation — the gate writes an act-scope `blocked` row with `사유=중단 답 라우팅 — 들인 답을 이 런에서 운반할 수 없다`, one per answer whichever of these causes it met, and exits 18. A halt of this run's own that the table does not hold — a base design's halt among them — is re-attached with `--resume` and a prompt that names this record's path and the chosen `선택지` verbatim. An implement stage's `좌석 실행` stop (a step beginning `Step 3 잔여 검증 좌석 실행` or `Step 3 게이트 도달 좌석 실행`) is re-attached that way directly, whether the seat ran the record or a person answered it: the carry act refuses an answer to it on its step alone. So is its seat-apply stop (a step beginning `Step 3 강제 표면 좌석 적용`) answered `좌석 적용` or `사람 몫으로 넘김`, whose resume prompt names the seat commit or the files left to a person; its `보류` parks the segment, and the carry act refuses all three on the step alone. A stage resumed that way takes the option as the answer to the question its record asked, continues from the step the record names, and does not redo a step it finished before halting; it does not rewrite the record. A fresh session handed a card enters the halted step from what is on disk, applies the answer there, writes a new halt record naming the card when the question it computes is not the record's, and does not rewrite the carried record either. When no account has room before the deadline, the answer is kept in an `이월` row for the next run, and the seat does not ask it again. The one routed exception is a composed requirement an audit's synthesis question asked about: answered `adopt as a requirement`, it goes to `design-reconverge` and then to a fresh audit rather than back into the halted session — the routing shift's skill states that path. An implement stage's binding-tier stop (CFI-U3 BT-STOP) answered `재수렴` is routed the same way, to `design-reconverge` and then to planning the segment again, and it is, with an answer an `이월` row names, the only answer a later run adopting the segment carries in when this run ended before handing it on: every other answer needs the halted session, and a session belongs to the run that dispatched it. An `이월` answer reaches the later run only through the carry act and its fresh session, never through `--resume`. The third exception is a review that stopped on an empty target (`review-unattended`'s `Step 1b 빈 대상`): a review session cannot make the commit the branch lacks, so `구현 재파견` dispatches the segment's implementation stage, whose `stage-result` row takes the answer up, and `세그먼트 park` writes the segment's `park` row, which takes it up — neither re-attaches the review. The fourth is a design stage's ladder halt answered `골격 재검토`: after the freeze it goes to `design-reconverge` with the scope `잔여 동결 재검토` (§3.2) and then a fresh audit.
- **The discriminator is the artifact, not the exit code.** A halt is a *clean* stop, so its terminal envelope looks like a normal completion, and a model-driven skill cannot set an exit code to mean otherwise. **Exit says the stage ended; the halt record says why.** Both are machine-read; neither is prose.
- **The volatile run directory is deliberately not durable.** Process handles — `stage-pid`, process-group id, transcript paths — live here and nowhere else, so a stale record and a stale process die together and pid reuse cannot make the driver kill an unrelated live process. It is under `XDG_STATE_HOME` rather than `${TMPDIR}` because `/var/folders` is swept without a reboot.

### 4.1 The audit's answer — resumed or re-entered

`design-audit-unattended` Reads this part when it is resumed with an answer to its own halt record, when it starts with `CC_PIPELINE_HALT_ANSWER` set, and before it writes a CFI-3b halt. It lives here rather than in that skill because the skill's own bytes count toward the ceiling of what its lead Reads before the first dispatch.

**Resumed in the same session.** A stage of that arm that halted can be resumed in the same session with a prompt naming its own halt record and one of the record's options verbatim (§4). The option is the answer to the question the record asked, and the pass goes on from the step the record names — the readers are not spawned again, collected witnesses are not re-collected, and the dispositions already applied stand. At the synthesis question: `record in the unresolved-issues section` records it as Step 6 records a candidate and goes on to Step 7; `reject` records the rejection in the report, with the answer's option as its basis, and goes on to Step 7. `adopt as a requirement` does not come back this way — the routing shift takes it to `design-reconverge` and a fresh audit, because the requirement changes what the run is trying to reach. At `Step 7 / 골격급 재발`: `미해결 이슈로 두고 계속` takes Step 7's disposition 4; `재수렴` goes the way `adopt as a requirement` does. Do not rewrite the halt record, and do not halt a second time on the question it already carries.

**Before a CFI-3b halt, leave the dispositions where a fresh session can take them.** Write a `## 조정 처분` block into the report at `<base>/docs/design-audit/{slug}.md`: one line per unique defect naming its severity and its owner (the document section, the carrier block or `## 미해결 이슈`), then `**조정 뒤 문서 sha256**: <64 hex>` — the design document's `shasum -a 256` after the last edit of this pass. Write it after the pass's own document edits and before the halt record. A session handed the person's answer to that halt skips Steps 1–5 only on this block (the re-entry below); without it, or with a sha256 the document no longer has, the next session audits again from a new freeze.

**Re-entered with a carried answer.** A fresh session can be handed a person's answer to an earlier attempt's synthesis halt (CFI-3b) — `record in the unresolved-issues section` or `reject`; `adopt as a requirement` still goes to `design-reconverge`. The routing shift's carry act writes an answer card and the stage starts with `CC_PIPELINE_HALT_ANSWER` set to its path. When it is set, before Step 1:

1. **Read the card and check the digest.** Its `**중단 기록**` is the earlier attempt's halt record and `**기록 다이제스트**` that record's sha256. Compute `shasum -a 256` of the record and compare; a difference is a halt with `분류: precondition-failed` naming both values — the answer was given to other bytes.
2. **Skip Steps 1–5 only on a disposition bound to the document's bytes.** The report at `<base>/docs/design-audit/{slug}.md` holds the evidence only when it carries the `## 조정 처분` block Step 6 writes before that halt and the block's `**조정 뒤 문서 sha256**` equals the design document's `shasum -a 256` now. Then the dispositions there stand, the report's `## 감사 개시` values are this pass's freeze, and the pass enters Step 6 at the synthesis question. Otherwise run Steps 1–5 again from a new freeze and Step 6 from its start: a report on disk does not vouch for the bytes it was made from, and only that sha256 binds it to them.
3. **Apply the card's option at the synthesis question** as the answer to the record's `**질문 문면**`, as the resume paragraph above describes, and go on to Step 7. Do not ask that question again.
4. **If the question computed there is not the record's** — a pass run again from Step 1 can compose a different requirement — do not apply the answer: write a new halt record for the question computed now, with the card's path in its `관측 상세`.
5. **Never rewrite the carried record** — the card's `**중단 기록**` belongs to the earlier attempt, and the answer is bound to its digest.

---

## 5. Termination recognition — the driver never parses prose

> **The driver's input from a stage is exactly two things — that the process ended, and what it left on disk. Prose output is never parsed for control flow: not the report body, not the Korean summary, not the next-step line.**

Skills do emit next-step command strings; the rule binds the **reader**, not the writer, which is why no skill text needs editing. Such a line is disqualified as a control signal anyway: it is emitted on the success path (so it cannot separate a finished audit from an aborted one) and it is cwd-relative (so it resolves against the wrong tree in a segment worktree). It is copied **verbatim as an opaque string** into the morning report, for the human who may run it.

**A stage's turn ends only when its own terminal artifact exists, when it has written a halt record, or when it has handed a judgment marker to the gate; prose is never a terminal, and a routing shift ends with its own skill's handoff.**

### 5.1 Artifact predicates

| Stage | Predicate |
| --- | --- |
| `design` | the freeze literal *"설계 문서를 동결했습니다. 이후 이 세션에서는 문서를 수정하지 않습니다."* |
| `design-audit` | the terminal literal *"이 명령은 여기서 종료합니다. 추가 리뷰 라운드는 없습니다."* + the `docs/design-audit/{slug}.reader-<k>.md` reader copies |
| `review` | the summary line `- **발견 요약**: 🔴 P0 N건 \| 🟠 P1 N건 \| 🟡 P2 N건 \| 🟢 P3 N건` in the report; the filename glob must accept the `review-pr{N}_{YYYY-MM-DD}[_v{N}].md` variants |
| `implement` | a git-state ladder — commit → branch ref → PR number, in the order the permission cutpoint authorizes. Evaluated by the driver **in the main tree** |
| `design-reconverge` | `docs/design-reconverge/{slug}.md` carrying `재수렴 sha256` and a two-value verdict (`재설계 필요` \| `불필요`), plus its confirming fixed literal. A terminal verdict returns to segment planning **unconditionally**, inside the same run — a moved document digest is recorded as a `문서 해시` row and needs no new run. Dispatched from the driver's ladder on a review finding, and by the routing shift on an implement stage's Step 1.5d or Step 3 refutation halt; either way the document argument is the **main-worktree absolute path**, because `docs/` is absent from every linked worktree |
| `design-base-unattended --split` (dispatched by the run) | the terminal literal *"베이스 분할을 마쳤습니다."* in the stage's own stream **and** a registry `docs/design-base/{slug}.tickets.md` whose head line starts `<!-- cc-design-base-tickets v1; `, whose `doc-sha256=` equals the document's current sha256, whose every `티켓` and `베이스` row reads `발행됨` (`문서만` when the frozen tracker is `없음`) and whose every `관계` row reads `걸림` or `문서만`. A halt record at `${RUN_DIR}/halt/<stage id>.md` makes it an intentional park |
| `design-discuss-unattended` (dispatched by the run) | the fixed literal *"설계 문서를 동결했습니다."* in the stage's own stream **and** a line reading exactly `**상태**: 동결됨` in the document it wrote. A halt record at `${RUN_DIR}/halt/<stage id>.md` makes it an intentional park. **One destination**: the stage emits the freeze literal, the document path and its whole-file `sha256`, then stops, naming no next step — the next step is the driver's or the router's graph, and a document that is not frozen goes to neither the audit nor segment planning |
